# Verification log

Every external fact the build depends on, checked against live sources. Per CLAUDE.md rule 7, any new external API shape or price gets a row here with the date checked.

Date of this pass: 2026-09-27. Method: two research agents fetching official pages where reachable, reputable secondary sources where login walled (marked).

## Confirmed: channel rules

| Item | Value | Source quality |
|---|---|---|
| Amazon main image | Pure white 255,255,255, product 85 percent or more, no text logos props watermarks, 1000 px zoom minimum, 1600 optimal, 10000 max, JPEG sRGB under 10 MB, up to 9 images, MAIN and PT01 to PT08 naming | Secondary (Seller Central G1881 is login walled). Recheck official wording once a seller account exists |
| Shopify product media | 5000 x 5000, 25 MP, under 20 MB, 2048 square recommended, GLB and USDZ 3D up to 500 MB, video 10 min 1 GB | Official help.shopify.com, exact match |
| Google Merchant IPTC | DigitalSourceType required for AI images. Three accepted values: TrainedAlgorithmicMedia, CompositeSynthetic, AlgorithmicMedia. Never strip embedded metadata. 500 x 500 minimum enforced 2027-01-31, 1500 x 1500 recommended. Rechecked 2026-10-01 for P18-09 part 2, unchanged: "All images created using generative AI must contain meta data indicating that the image was AI-generated (for example, the IPTC DigitalSourceTypeTrainedAlgorithmicMedia metadata tag)"; "Don't remove embedded metadata tags such as the IPTC DigitalSourceType property from images created using generative AI tools"; the three values are listed as "TrainedAlgorithmicMedia: The image was created using a model derived from sampled content", "CompositeSynthetic: The image is a composite that includes synthetic elements" and "AlgorithmicMedia: The image was created purely by an algorithm not based on any sampled training data". The IPTC vocabulary (cv.iptc.org/newscodes/digitalsourcetype, fetched 2026-10-01) lists compositeSynthetic as current, URI http://cv.iptc.org/newscodes/digitalsourcetype/compositeSynthetic, "Mix or composite of several elements, at least one of which is Generative AI", the value packages/pipeline/src/metadata/iptc.ts writes on composited scenes. Whether production files carry it is still unconfirmed until the founder runs `pnpm --filter @curvi/trigger smoke:iptc <object key>` on one delivered lifestyle file (PHASE_18 founder step 10) | Official support.google.com/merchants/answer/6324350 (rechecked 2026-10-01); cv.iptc.org/newscodes/digitalsourcetype (2026-10-01) |
| IPTC tag in PNG and WebP | ExifTool's supported file types table: JPEG "R/W" with XMP "R/W/C"; PNG "R/W" with XMP "R/W/C"; WEBP "R/W" with XMP "R/W/C" and no IPTC IIM support. DigitalSourceType is written as XMP-iptcExt, so all three delivered formats can carry it. Round trips for all three (write composite and trained, read back, clear) pass with the installed exiftool-vendored 28.8.0 (packages/pipeline/src/metadata/iptc.test.ts), and buildPack's delivered JPEG, PNG and WebP scenes read back as composite (packages/pipeline/src/packager/iptc-formats.test.ts) | Official exiftool.org supported file types table (fetched 2026-10-01); local tests |
| Shopify CDN IPTC stripping | Was real (community thread May to Sept 2026); Shopify staff say fixed as of 2026-07-27. | Add an empirical runtime check in Phase 8: upload tagged image via stagedUploadsCreate, fetch from CDN, assert tag survives. Keep the cdn.curvi.ai feed fallback in the design |
| Amazon SP API images | patchListingsItem with main_product_image_locator, other_product_image_locator_1 to 8 confirmed on official docs. $1,400 annual fee cancelled May 2026. Non Amazon CDN URLs can be rejected: serve publish copies from a fetchable public bucket URL | Official developer docs |
| Shopify app economics | 0 percent on first $1M lifetime revenue (from 2025-01-01), 15 percent above, $19 one time registration, PLUS a 2.9 percent processing fee on all billing. New apps should target Shopify App Pricing rather than the legacy Billing API | Official shopify.dev |
| eBay | 500 px minimum longest side, 1600 recommended, no watermarks, borders or added text | Official ebay.com help |
| Etsy | 2000 px shortest side recommended, 4:3 or 1:1 | Official help.etsy.com |

## Build environment decisions (2026-09-27)

- Registry now serves TypeScript 7, ESLint 10, Vitest 5, Next 16, Stripe SDK 22, Trigger.dev SDK 4. Pinned instead: TypeScript 5.9, Next 15.5, ESLint 9, Vitest 3, Zod 4, Drizzle 0.44, Trigger.dev SDK 3, sharp 0.34, exiftool-vendored 28. Reason: known good combination; upgrades are deliberate follow ups.
- pnpm 10 ignores dependency build scripts by default; sharp and esbuild load via prebuilt platform binaries, verified working on this machine (vips 8.17.3, exiftool 13.00).
- sharp 0.34 morphology semantics for white foreground masks are inverted on this platform; packages/pipeline/src/mask.ts documents and pins this with tests.

Each adapter carries a docstring flag. Record dates here when first exercised with real keys.

## Security audit (2026-09-27)

A read only security review of wave 1 executed exploit probes against the repo's own PGlite harness and composite pipeline. It confirmed 3 critical RLS privilege escalations (member self grant of credits, PUBLIC executable SECURITY DEFINER ledger functions, client role workspace update and delete), 4 high findings (subscriptions self upgrade, unprotected recipes and channel_specs tables, thin product fidelity bypass through mask erosion, silently bypassable cost caps) and 9 medium or low findings. All 16 were fixed the same day: migration 0002_security_hardening.sql (19 regression tests that re-run the exploits and assert denial), fail closed cost estimation in packages/ai, adaptive paste erosion plus a derived QC erosion invariant and a maxDeltaE guard in packages/pipeline, filename sanitization in packages/specs, an events dedupe unique index (migration 0003) making webhook grants atomic, and a fal poll URL allowlist. Ledger SQL functions are callable only by the service role or the owner connection; the web app was reconciled to call them over DATABASE_URL.

## Full audit and P0 remediation (2026-09-27, second pass)

A 22 agent audit compared all 257 plan requirements against the code; docs/AUDIT_2026-09-27.md holds the verified findings. The three P0 findings were fixed the same day:

1. Web to worker bridge: POST /api/jobs now enqueues generate-pack through the Trigger.dev SDK when TRIGGER_SECRET_KEY is set, and otherwise runs the pack inline after the response through next/server after() with the same runner (apps/web/src/lib/jobs/enqueue.ts). A crashed inline run marks the job failed and releases the hold.
2. DB credit settlement: DbJobStore (trigger/src/db-store.ts) persists job state, assets, job_steps, and calls charge_credits per passing asset with the shot id as step key and release_credits per failed shot and remainder. Migration 0004 and 0005 move the ledger to numeric(12,1) so the plan's 0.5 credit deterministic assets bill exactly, and release_credits gained an exact amount parameter. Tested end to end against the real migrations in trigger/src/db-store.test.ts.
3. Pack delivery: buildPack writes loose per channel files, DbJobStore uploads files, zips and the compliance report to R2 under ws/{workspace}/jobs/{job}/, records asset_variants and pack_files rows (new table, member read RLS), and GET /api/jobs/:id/files serves 15 minute signed urls rendered as channel tabs with named downloads and previews (PackDownloads component).

Also landed in the same pass: uploads are now recorded (POST /api/uploads/complete writes source_media; the pack form registers each upload and auto creates a product), Listing Mode refuses to start without a real photo, the client role can no longer create jobs, products or uploads through the app layer, IPTC DigitalSourceType is embedded at package time (composite for composited stills, trained for fully generated and all concept outputs, none for deterministic edits), concept packs structurally exclude marketplace channels in the runner, the rule 3 fidelity gate fails closed when a composite generation omits its product reference or mask, the compliance badge carries measured fill and background from the worker, checkout.session.completed writes workspaces.stripe_customer_id back, and pnpm db:seed (trigger/src/seed-cli.ts) seeds channel specs and recipes into the tables.

Waves 2 and 3 (same day): provision_workspace (migration 0007) creates the first workspace, owner membership and the free 15 credit grant exactly once per user; the runner blocks flagged uploads and flagged products before generation; seller text reaches the LLMs inside user_description tags; generation_jobs stores channels and mode so idempotency replays verify the whole body and cross workspace conflicts stop leaking job ids; every pricing number on the marketing surface derives from packages/pipeline/seed. Spend caps are live on the call path: layered caps hooks in callWithFailover (pack plus global day on every LLM call), per asset, pack and global reservations on each shot generation with the cost capped needs review branch, the onSpendAlert hook at the $50 line, and DAILY_SPEND_HARD_STOP_USD raising the $150 stop. The Anthropic adapter honors the recipe selected model with per model price tables, failing closed for unpriced models.

Merge and production migration (2026-09-28): the deploy branch (worktree-ui-update-plan, previously origin/main) was merged into main. Migration numbers were reconciled: 0004_signup_bootstrap and 0005_brand_logo stay, main's migrations became 0006 to 0009 (so provision_workspace is now 0009). Production Supabase (tmwvjmvzjvpeagatjmud) was checked read only and found at 0005 with no drizzle.__drizzle_migrations table. 0006 to 0009 were applied in one guarded transaction through the SQL editor, and all ten migrations were recorded in drizzle.__drizzle_migrations with sha256 hashes and journal timestamps, so pnpm db:migrate treats production as current. Verified afterwards: pack_files with RLS and its member read policy, numeric(12,1) ledger, generation_jobs channels and mode, numeric ledger functions and provision_workspace executable by service_role only, existing ledger balance unchanged. Functional fix plan from the post merge audit: Update.md.

Live pack safety (2026-09-28, Update.md 2.1, 5.2, 1.6, 3.1), done because production has live Anthropic, Gemini, BFL and Photoroom keys:
- Live mode never substitutes demo images.
- Provider spend is reserved against caps before each live call.
- Credits are charged only after the pack is stored.
- The worker cannot revive a job the stale reconciler failed; the reconciler releases credits only when it wins a conditional update.
- Tests cover each path in trigger/src/live-runtime.test.ts, pipeline-runner.test.ts, db-store.test.ts (real SQL on PGlite) and apps/web/src/lib/services/db.test.ts.
- The reviewer agent confirmed settlement in every path. It then found that spend caps were per process, and that a database-backed run with no keys still charged for demo output. Both are fixed:
  - Migration 0010 adds spend_cap_counters: platform table, RLS on, no client privileges. PgCapStore (trigger/src/cap-store.ts, cap-store.test.ts) is shared across runs.
  - Database-backed runs never use the demo generator unless CURVI_ALLOW_DEMO_GENERATION=1.
- Migration 0010 must be applied to production before the code that uses it is deployed.

## Still template font and text rendering (checked 2026-09-28)

- Font: @expo-google-fonts/inter 0.4.2, added to packages/pipeline. npm license field "MIT AND OFL-1.1": package code MIT, Inter font files SIL Open Font License 1.1 (LICENSE_FONT ships in the package). Verified after install that the package ships TTF files, for example 600SemiBold/Inter_600SemiBold.ttf, which the still template renderer uses. The font's name table family is "Inter SemiBold".
- Text shaping: opentype.js 1.3.4 (MIT, deps tiny-inflate and string.prototype.codepointat), with @types/opentype.js 1.3.10 (MIT) as a dev dependency. Glyph outlines are converted to SVG paths and rasterized by sharp (librsvg), so no system font stack is involved.
- Why not sharp text input with fontfile: with sharp 0.34.5 (Pango 1.57, fontconfig 2.17.1) on macOS the fontfile option was ignored and output fell back to a Helvetica like system face, and Linux hosts do not guarantee fonts. Glyph paths from the bundled TTF render identically on every host.
- Resolution: packages/pipeline/src/templates/font.ts resolves the TTF with createRequire from the module, then from the working directory, then by walking up node_modules layouts. CURVI_TEMPLATE_FONT_FILE overrides the path for hosts whose bundler does not ship node_modules files (a Trigger.dev cloud deploy bundles code and may not include the TTF; set the env var or keep the package external there).

## SEO, GEO and AEO metadata (checked 2026-09-28)

Marketing metadata lives in apps/web/src/lib/seo.ts (titles, descriptions, canonical URLs, social cards, JSON-LD) and apps/web/src/lib/llms.ts (/llms.txt). An independent review checked these facts at official sources:

| Item | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| OpenAI crawler tokens | GPTBot (training), OAI-SearchBot (ChatGPT search), ChatGPT-User (user actions; robots.txt may not apply) | developers.openai.com/api/docs/bots |
| Anthropic crawler tokens | ClaudeBot (training), Claude-SearchBot (search quality), Claude-User (user fetches); all respect robots.txt | support.claude.com article 8896518 |
| Perplexity crawler tokens | PerplexityBot (search, respects robots.txt), Perplexity-User (user fetches, generally ignores robots.txt) | docs.perplexity.ai/guides/bots |
| Google-Extended | Control token only, no crawler of its own; governs Gemini training and grounding | developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/google-common-crawlers |
| Apple | Applebot crawls for Spotlight, Siri and Safari; Applebot-Extended never crawls, it only controls model training use | support.apple.com/en-us/119829 |
| Amazon | Amazonbot (product improvement, may train models), Amzn-SearchBot (search in Amazon products), Amzn-User (user actions) | developer.amazon.com/amazonbot |
| Meta | meta-webindexer (Meta AI search), meta-externalagent (training), meta-externalfetcher (user fetches, may bypass robots.txt) | developers.facebook.com/docs/sharing/webmasters/web-crawlers |
| DuckDuckGo and Common Crawl | DuckAssistBot (AI answers, not used for training); CCBot honors robots.txt | duckduckgo.com help pages; commoncrawl.org/ccbot |
| FAQ rich results | Google stopped showing FAQ rich results on 2026-05-07. FAQPage markup stays valid schema.org and is kept for answer engines | developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/structured-data/faqpage |
| Software app rich result | Requires name, offers.price and aggregateRating or review. Curvi has no ratings and must not invent them, so it will not get the rich result; the markup still describes the product | developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/structured-data/software-app |
| schema.org | Every type and property used in seo.ts exists and is current | schema.org/version/latest |
| llms.txt format | H1, optional blockquote, body without headings, then H2 sections that are lists of [name](url) links | llmstxt.org |
| Next.js 15.5.26 behavior | A page that sets openGraph drops the root opengraph-image, so pageMetadata sets the image explicitly; dynamic params arrive percent encoded; child robots metadata replaces the parent's | installed next source under apps/web/node_modules/next/dist |

Honesty rules applied: SoftwareApplication offers list paid plans only when STRIPE_SECRET_KEY is set, and FAQPage markup includes only answers that describe shipped features (see the structured flags in the home page and help page).

### Still unverified (batch 1)

1. **Harmonize output sizes behind the aspect tolerance.** `HARMONIZE_ASPECT_TOLERANCE = 0.04` (packages/pipeline/src/composite/index.ts) is the implementer's own figure. Neither the Gemini API reference nor the image generation guide lists output pixel sizes per aspect ratio (the guide at ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/image-generation was rechecked on 2026-09-28: it lists the ratios and the 512, 1K, 2K and 4K sizes, but no per ratio pixel table), and no BFL output size table was checked. The outcome depends on those sizes. Example: shopify.hero_banner is 2400x1000 (2.4:1) and its nearest Gemini ratio is 21:9. An output of 1584x672 drifts 1.8 percent and passes; 1536x672 drifts 4.8 percent and the shot fails. To close: at the first live key, run a harmonize call for a 1:1 and a 2.4:1 canvas on each image provider in the chain (the Gemini and BFL models in the seed), record width x height here with the date, and set the tolerance from those figures.
2. **Client IP header on Render behind Cloudflare.** `clientIp()` in apps/web/src/lib/rate-limit.ts trusts `cf-connecting-ip`, then `true-client-ip`, then `x-real-ip`, then the first `X-Forwarded-For` entry, and its comment says a Cloudflare edge overwrites any value the client sent. Cloudflare's HTTP headers reference (developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/reference/http-headers/, rechecked 2026-09-28) says CF-Connecting-IP carries the IP of the client connecting to Cloudflare; that True-Client-IP is the same value under another name, available only on the Enterprise plan through the "Add True-Client-IP header" Managed Transform; and that Cloudflare appends to an existing X-Forwarded-For rather than replacing it, so its first entry can be whatever the client sent. The page does not say whether Cloudflare replaces a CF-Connecting-IP value sent by the client. Nothing checked says what Render's own edge sets or strips, or whether curvi.ai traffic passes through a Cloudflare proxy before Render. Until this is tested, treat the per IP limit as spoofable; the per user limits still hold. To close: from outside, send requests with forged `cf-connecting-ip`, `true-client-ip`, `x-real-ip` and `x-forwarded-for` headers to a production route that logs the request headers it receives, record which values arrive, and make `clientIp()` trust only headers the edge sets.
3. **Whether BFL and fal charge for moderated or failed jobs.** Neither documents it. The adapters treat any failure after a successful create or submit as billed at the per image price (an upper bound for the spend caps). Confirm at the first live call.
4. **Stripe dispute lifecycle (closed by the fix pass, see the table above).** The P2 review proposed returning clawed back credits on `charge.dispute.closed` with status `won` or on `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`, and skipping inquiries (statuses starting with `warning_`). None of these event names or statuses was checked against Stripe docs in batch 1; check them before building that handler.
5. **Redis script atomicity.** The Upstash limiter relies on EVAL running its INCR plus PEXPIRE script atomically. That is standard Redis scripting behavior but was not checked against a source in batch 1.
6. **`RENDER_GIT_COMMIT`.** /api/health reports it as `commit`, on the assumption that Render sets it at runtime. Not checked in batch 1; the first batch 1 deploy confirms it when `commit` is not null.
7. **Adapter request shapes.** The VERIFY AT FIRST LIVE CALL items listed under "Unverified adapter endpoint shapes" above are still open (anthropic-version value and field set, Gemini request casing, BFL create path, OpenAI response encoding, Photoroom multipart contract, fal queue fields).

## 2026-09-28: LLM structured output and image memory

- Anthropic structured outputs (platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs): strict tool use is `"strict": true` on a tool definition, no beta header. Supported on claude-haiku-4-5-20251001 and claude-sonnet-5 among others. Strict schemas reject numeric bounds (minimum, maximum, multipleOf), string bounds (minLength, maxLength, pattern) and recursive schemas; objects need `additionalProperties: false`; array minItems supports only 0 and 1. The SDKs strip these and validate client side; our raw HTTP adapter does the same through strictToolSchema plus Zod.
- sharp (sharp.pixelplumbing.com/api-utility, /install): `sharp.cache()` defaults to 50 MB, 20 files, 100 items and `false` removes caching. `sharp.concurrency()` defaults to the CPU count, except 1 on glibc Linux without jemalloc.
- Image adapters audited against current docs the same day (Gemini generateContent, OpenAI Images, BFL FLUX.2, Photoroom segment, fal queue): no call shape errors. Fixes applied: BFL "Failed" is terminal, Photoroom sends a typed file with Accept image/png, fal errors reported on COMPLETED are raised, Gemini interim thought images are skipped. Still open: OpenAI harmonize could use /v1/images/edits; BFL edit pricing above 1.33 MP is metered low; the gpt-image-1 shutdown date is now 2026-12-01.

| sharp `limitInputPixels`: "Do not process input images where the number of pixels (width x height) exceeds this limit", default 268402689. sharp refuses even `metadata()` of an image over the limit, so the ingest reads the header with the limit lifted and applies the 80 MP cap itself (tested). | packages/pipeline/src/ingest/image.ts | sharp.pixelplumbing.com/api-constructor; behavior of installed sharp 0.34.5 | Implementer (b2/trust) |
| The prebuilt sharp binaries support "JPEG, PNG, Ultra HDR, WebP, AVIF, TIFF, GIF and SVG (input)"; HEIC is not listed, so HEIC uploads are recognized by their ftyp brand and refused with a plain notice. | packages/pipeline/src/ingest/image.ts | sharp.pixelplumbing.com/install | Implementer (b2/trust), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| WebP container: VP8X flag byte bits are Rsv(2) I L E X A R, so EXIF is 0x08 and XMP 0x04; metadata chunks are `EXIF` and `XMP ` (trailing space); odd sized chunks carry one zero padding byte; the RIFF size counts from offset 8. | packages/pipeline/src/ingest/image.ts stripWebpMetadata | developers.google.com/speed/webp/docs/riff_container | Implementer (b2/trust), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| PNG chunks are length (4), type (4), data, CRC (4), with the CRC over type and data only, so dropping whole chunks leaves the rest valid. tEXt, zTXt, iTXt, tIME and eXIf are ancillary. | packages/pipeline/src/ingest/image.ts stripPngMetadata | w3.org/TR/png-3/ | Implementer (b2/trust), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| QuickTime and MP4 atoms: a 32 bit size including the header, then the type; size 1 means a 64 bit extended size follows the type; size 0 (top level only) runs to the end of the file. The movie header `mvhd` holds version (1), flags (3), creation (4), modification (4), time scale (4) and duration (4) in version 0. | packages/pipeline/src/ingest/video.ts | developer.apple.com/documentation/quicktime-file-format (Atoms; Movie header atom) | Implementer (b2/trust), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| `mvhd` version 1 uses 64 bit creation, modification and duration fields (time scale stays 32 bit). Apple's page documents version 0 only. | packages/pipeline/src/ingest/video.ts mvhdDurationSeconds | ISO/IEC 14496-12 (ISO base media file format), not fetched: the standard is paywalled | Implementer (b2/trust); unverified against the text, covered by a synthetic fixture test |

### Phase 12 A5: intake screenshot flag (recorded 2026-09-28)

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| A strict tool (`strict: true`) may leave properties out of `required`: the official quick start sends `unit` as an optional property next to a required `location`, with `additionalProperties: false`, and the only guarantee stated is that the tool `input` "strictly follows the `input_schema`". So the optional `screenshot` boolean in IntakeImageResult is accepted in strict mode, and the model may still omit it; the runner reads a missing value as not a screenshot, and intake version 2's prompt asks for it on every image. No limit on the number of optional properties is stated on either page. | packages/pipeline/src/schemas.ts IntakeImageResult, strictToolSchema; trigger/src/pipeline-runner.ts | platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/strict-tool-use; platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs (JSON Schema limitations) | Implementer (p12/intake-screenshot), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| Supported in strict schemas: the basic types including boolean, `enum`, `const`, `anyOf`, `required`, `additionalProperties: false` (required on every object), array `minItems` of 0 or 1, and the listed string formats. Numeric and string constraints are not supported. Unchanged from the earlier strictToolSchema check. | packages/pipeline/src/schemas.ts | platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs | Implementer (p12/intake-screenshot), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, intake's primary model, and `claude-sonnet-5`, its fallback, are both on the structured outputs supported model list. | packages/pipeline/src/seed/recipes.ts intake_normalizer version 2 | platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs | Implementer (p12/intake-screenshot), fetched 2026-09-28 |

### Account deletion with a plan set to end (fix/pipeline-billing, recorded 2026-09-28)

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cancel a subscription: `DELETE /v1/subscriptions/:id` (SDK `stripe.subscriptions.cancel(id, params, options)`). It cancels immediately and the customer is not charged again. Optional params: `cancellation_details` (`comment`, `feedback`), `invoice_now` (final invoice for unbilled metered usage and pending prorations, default false) and `prorate` (credit for unused time, default false). Returns the Subscription with `status: "canceled"`. With both false, pending prorations are removed. | apps/web/src/lib/trust/account.ts deleteAccountData | docs.stripe.com/api/subscriptions/cancel; installed stripe 18.5.0 types (SubscriptionCancelParams) | Implementer (fix/pipeline-billing), fetched 2026-09-28 |
| Subscription object: `cancel_at_period_end` (boolean) says whether it will cancel at the end of the current period; `cancel_at` (timestamp, nullable) is a future date at which it will be canceled automatically. Either one means Stripe ends the subscription on its own, so both count as set to end. Status values are `incomplete`, `incomplete_expired`, `trialing`, `active`, `past_due`, `canceled`, `unpaid`, `paused`. | account.ts subscriptionIsEnding; cancel-service.ts readSubscriptionFacts; stripe-webhook.ts subscriptionState | docs.stripe.com/api/subscriptions/object; installed stripe 18.5.0 types (`cancel_at: number \| null`, `cancel_at_period_end: boolean`) | Implementer (fix/pipeline-billing), fetched 2026-09-28 |

### Duplicate subscription protection (fix/duplicate-subscriptions, recorded 2026-09-29)

API version in use: `2025-08-27.basil` (apps/web/src/lib/billing/stripe.ts), stripe-node 18.5.0.

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Create a customer: `POST /v1/customers` takes optional `email` (up to 512 characters) and `metadata` (key value pairs). | apps/web/src/lib/billing/checkout-guard.ts ensureStripeCustomer | docs.stripe.com/api/customers/create; installed stripe 18.5.0 types | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Idempotency: every `POST` accepts an idempotency key (up to 255 characters). Stripe saves the status code and body of the first request for a key, success or failure, and returns it for later requests with the same key; keys may be pruned once at least 24 hours old; reusing a key with different parameters errors; a request that conflicts with a concurrent request on the same key is not saved and can be retried (error code `idempotency_key_in_use`). Keys should not contain personal data, so the customer key is the workspace id only. | checkout-guard.ts `curvi-customer-<workspaceId>`; stripe.ts `curvi-dup-refund-<subscriptionId>` | docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests; docs.stripe.com/error-codes | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| List Checkout Sessions: `GET /v1/checkout/sessions` filters by `customer` and by `status` (`open`, `complete`, `expired`); `limit` 1 to 100, default 10. | checkout-guard.ts openTierCheckout | docs.stripe.com/api/checkout/sessions/list; installed stripe 18.5.0 types (SessionListParams) | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Expire a Checkout Session: `POST /v1/checkout/sessions/:id/expire`, only for status `open`. Afterwards the customer cannot complete it and sees a message that it has expired. Errors if the session has already expired or is not in an expirable state. | checkout-guard.ts openTierCheckout | docs.stripe.com/api/checkout/sessions/expire | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| List subscriptions: `status` accepts `active`, `all`, `canceled`, `ended`, `incomplete`, `incomplete_expired`, `past_due`, `paused`, `trialing`, `unpaid`; with no value, all subscriptions that are not canceled are returned; `all` returns every status. Filters by `customer`; `limit` 1 to 100. The list items carry `created`, `customer`, `status` and `latest_invoice`. | checkout-guard.ts openTierCheckout; stripe.ts createStripeBillingActions.listCustomerSubscriptions | docs.stripe.com/api/subscriptions/list; installed stripe 18.5.0 types (SubscriptionListParams.Status) | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Cancel a subscription: cancels immediately, no further charges. `invoice_now` (default false) would create a final invoice for pending prorations; `prorate` (default false) would credit unused time. With both false pending prorations are removed. | stripe.ts cancelSubscription (`invoice_now: false, prorate: false`) | docs.stripe.com/api/subscriptions/cancel | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Create a refund: `POST /v1/refunds` needs a `charge` or a `payment_intent`; `amount` defaults to the whole unrefunded amount; `reason` is one of `duplicate`, `fraudulent`, `requested_by_customer`; `metadata` allowed. Refunding an already refunded charge errors with code `charge_already_refunded`. | stripe.ts refundInvoice | docs.stripe.com/api/refunds/create; docs.stripe.com/error-codes | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Since `2025-03-31.basil` the Invoice object has no `payment_intent`, `charge` or `paid`; payments are InvoicePayment objects. `GET /v1/invoice_payments` filters by `invoice` and `status` (`paid`, `open`, `canceled`); each has `amount_paid`, `currency`, `status` and `payment` with `type` (`payment_intent` or `charge`) and `payment_intent` or `charge`. | stripe.ts refundInvoice | docs.stripe.com/changelog/basil/2025-03-31/add-support-for-multiple-partial-payments-on-invoices; docs.stripe.com/api/invoice-payment/list; installed stripe 18.5.0 types (InvoicePayment.Payment) | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Customer Portal: prices with the same `product` and `recurring.interval` cannot both be offered, so each tier needs its own product with one monthly and one yearly price; at most 10 products in the plan switcher. "Manage downgrades" can only schedule a change at the period end between prices of the same product. | docs/STRIPE_SETUP.md sections 1 and 5 | docs.stripe.com/customer-management ("Limitations"); docs.stripe.com/customer-management/configure-portal | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| `pg_advisory_xact_lock` holds until the transaction ends; `lock_timeout` bounds the wait. | checkout-guard.ts withCheckoutLock | PostgreSQL documentation (advisory lock functions, lock_timeout), from memory, not fetched; the lock SQL runs in checkout-guard.test.ts against PGlite | Implementer (fix/duplicate-subscriptions) |

### Home liquid metal hero, review fixes on home/liquid-metal-final (recorded 2026-09-29)

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| ShaderMount sets `data-paper-shader` and `paperShaderMount` on its div only at the end of its constructor, after `processUniforms` has awaited the u_image decode; the first draw happens in `handleResize` from the ResizeObserver, right after the canvas gets its size. So "mount exists and canvas width above 0" means a frame has been drawn. The library also puts `data-paper-shader` on the `<style>` it prepends to head, so page wide lookups must use `div[data-paper-shader]`. | components/ui/liquid-metal-canvas.tsx (useFirstFrame); e2e/home-hero.spec.ts | @paper-design/shaders 0.0.81 dist/shader-mount.js | Implementer (home/liquid-metal-final), 2026-09-29 |
| `setSpeed(0)` (the React `speed` prop, applied in an effect) sets `currentSpeed` to 0 and cancels the requestAnimationFrame loop; the IntersectionObserver and visibilitychange handlers keep `currentSpeed` at 0 while the element is offscreen or the tab hidden, whatever the speed. `setFrame(ms)` renders that frame at once. | components/ui/liquid-metal-backdrop.tsx (motion toggle); e2e/home-hero.spec.ts | @paper-design/shaders 0.0.81 dist/shader-mount.js; shaders-react dist/shader-mount.js | Implementer (home/liquid-metal-final), 2026-09-29 |
| WCAG 2.2.2: "For any moving, blinking or scrolling information that (1) starts automatically, (2) lasts more than five seconds, and (3) is presented in parallel with other content, there is a mechanism for the user to pause, stop, or hide it". A reduced motion media query is not such a mechanism, hence the pause control. | components/ui/liquid-metal-backdrop.tsx LiquidMetalMotionToggle | w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/pause-stop-hide | Implementer (home/liquid-metal-final), 2026-09-29 |
| WCAG 1.4.11: user interface components need 3:1 against adjacent colors, and a focus indicator outside the component must contrast with the background it sits on. The old burgundy ring #8f2749 is 2.4:1 on night; #d0587a is 5.1:1 on night and 3.9:1 on white. | packages/ui button.tsx secondary variant | w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-contrast | Implementer (home/liquid-metal-final), 2026-09-29 |
| `overflow: hidden` makes the element a scroll container; `overflow: clip` does not, and `overflow-x: clip` with `overflow-y: visible` stays clip. A scroll container ancestor is what `view()` timelines and `position: sticky` track, so the home wrapper uses overflow-x-clip. | apps/web/src/app/(marketing)/page.tsx | developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/overflow | Implementer (home/liquid-metal-final), 2026-09-29 |

**sharp 0.34.5 color handling (installed in packages/pipeline/node_modules/sharp)**

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default output: "The default behaviour, when `keepMetadata` is not used, is to convert to the device-independent sRGB colour space and strip all metadata, including the removal of any ICC profile." `keepIccProfile()` keeps the input profile ("where possible") and skips the conversion; `withIccProfile(name or path, { attach })` transforms to that profile and attaches it unless `attach: false`; built in names srgb, p3, cmyk. `withMetadata()` adds "a web-friendly sRGB ICC profile if appropriate". The constructor option `ignoreIcc` skips the embedded profile. Probe: an 8 bit JPEG tagged Display P3 holding sRGB red (stored as 234, 51, 35) came out of a default `jpeg()` as 255, 1, 3 with no profile; `keepIccProfile()` kept 234, 51, 35 and the P3 profile; `withIccProfile('srgb')` gave 255, 1, 3 with an sRGB profile; `ignoreIcc` gave 234, 51, 35 untransformed. | kept photo color conversion (P0 original.ts; whiten.ts buildProductReferenceFromEncoded) | sharp.pixelplumbing.com/api-output; installed lib/output.js (keepMetadata JSDoc), lib/constructor.js (ignoreIcc) | Implementer (p15/verify), fetched and run 2026-09-29 |
| Rendering intent: every ICC transform sharp makes (embedded profile to the processing space, CMYK to RGB, and `withIccProfile`) is hard coded to `VIPS_INTENT_PERCEPTUAL`; the API has no option to change it. | same | installed src/pipeline.cc lines 342 to 355 and 829 to 833 | Implementer (p15/verify), read 2026-09-29 |
| Reading the profile: `metadata()` returns `hasProfile` (boolean), `icc` (Buffer of the raw profile), `space`, `channels`, `depth`. sharp does not return the profile's description; read it from the `icc` buffer's `desc` tag (ICC v2 type `desc`, ASCII; ICC v4 type `mluc`, UTF-16BE records). The probe parsed sharp's own built in profiles this way: srgb reads "sRGB", p3 reads "sP3C". | P0 originalFit.srgbProfileNames | sharp.pixelplumbing.com/api-input (metadata); installed lib/index.d.ts lines 1250 to 1257 | Implementer (p15/verify), run 2026-09-29 |
| CMYK input: with an embedded profile sharp transforms through it; without one it uses its built in "cmyk" input profile (perceptual). Default output is 3 channel sRGB with no profile. Probe: a 4 channel CMYK JPEG with no profile came out of `png()` as `space` srgb, 3 channels, no profile. | same | installed src/pipeline.cc lines 333 to 356 | Implementer (p15/verify), run 2026-09-29 |
| 16 bit input to 8 bit output: the default output is 8 bit (`depth` uchar) for JPEG and also for PNG; 16 bit PNG output needs `toColourspace('rgb16')` ("For PNG 16-bit output, convert to rgb16"). Probe: a 16 bit PNG came out of `png()` and `jpeg()` as uchar. | same | sharp.pixelplumbing.com/api-output; installed src/pipeline.cc lines 796 to 800 | Implementer (p15/verify), run 2026-09-29 |
| Trap, 16 bit input with an embedded profile: sharp picks Display P3, not sRGB, as the processing profile for 16 bit RGB input (`processingProfile = RGB16 ? "p3" : "srgb"`), and the default output neither converts back nor tags it. Probe: a 16 bit PNG with an embedded sRGB profile holding pure red came out of a default `jpeg()` as 234, 51, 35 with no profile, a visibly duller red read as sRGB. The same pixels without a profile, or the tagged file with `withIccProfile('srgb', { attach: false })`, came out as 254, 0, 0. Any kept or reference path that can see a 16 bit PNG or TIFF with a profile must call `withIccProfile('srgb')` (attach true or false) instead of relying on the default, and the reference and the shipped file must use the same call. | same; rule 3 fidelity of kept outputs | installed src/pipeline.cc line 332; scratch probe against installed sharp 0.34.5 | Implementer (p15/verify), run 2026-09-29 |
| `resize` `fastShrinkOnLoad` (default true): "Take greater advantage of the JPEG and WebP shrink-on-load feature, which can lead to a slight moiré pattern or round-down of an auto-scaled dimension." With false, sharp leaves at least a factor of two for the final kernel resize (`factor = fastShrinkOnLoad ? 1 : 2`). Only JPEG and WebP loads are affected. **Trigger.dev task limits and the subtask boundary**

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Limits page: "Single trigger payload: Must not exceed 3MB"; "Task outputs: Must not exceed 10MB"; batch trigger payload "Each item can be up to 3MB (SDK 4.3.1+). Prior: 1MB total combined". Payloads and outputs over 512KB are kept in object storage; the SDK uploads anything over 128KB before sending. The page carries only v4 era notes; /docs/v3/limits serves the same content. | trigger/src/tasks/generate-shot.ts output (SerializableShotOutcome) | trigger.dev/docs/limits; trigger.dev/docs/v3/limits | Implementer (p15/verify), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Installed @trigger.dev/sdk and core 3.3.17 offload any IO packet of 128 KiB or more (`OFFLOAD_IO_PACKET_LENGTH_LIMIT = 128 * 1024`), so large outputs travel through object storage, but the 10MB output cap still applies. A 20 MB original is about 26.7 MB as base64, and an 8 MB 16 MP JPEG is about 10.7 MB, so one kept original can exceed the cap on its own. Per PHASE_15 item 15, a kept original crossing the subtask boundary goes to R2 under the workspace prefix and passes the key. | trigger/src/pipeline-runner.ts SerializedPackFile | installed @trigger.dev/core 3.3.17 dist/commonjs/v3/limits.js and utils/ioSerialization.js | Implementer (p15/verify), read 2026-09-29 |

Only the generate-pack task passes runShots (generateShot.batchTriggerAndWait); pack-follow-up and the inline follow up path never cross the boundary. docs/LAUNCH_CHECKLIST.md step 14 keeps TRIGGER_SECRET_KEY unset on Render until the v4 upgrade (v3 no longer runs on Trigger.dev Cloud, row above). So production packs run inline today, and the boundary matters once the v4 cloud deploy lands. The Render environment itself was not read. | apps/web/src/lib/jobs/enqueue.ts; trigger/src/tasks/generate-pack.ts, generate-shot.ts; pipeline-runner.ts runShots | repository at p15/output-options | Implementer (p15/verify), read 2026-09-29 |

| fal BiRefNet v2 variants | 2026-09-29 | https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/birefnet/v2/api | model: General Use (Light), General Use (Light 2K), General Use (Heavy), Matting, Portrait, General Use (Dynamic). operating_resolution 1024x1024, 2048x2048, or 2304x2304 (Dynamic only). refine_foreground (default true) refines the foreground from the estimated mask; output_mask and mask_only return the mask. **Model Context Protocol, Streamable HTTP**

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| The current protocol version is `2026-07-28`. Earlier handshake based revisions (`2025-11-25` and before) used `initialize`; 2026-07-28 removed protocol level sessions and the GET stream. Every request carries its version in `params._meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion"]`, and clients may call `server/discover` to learn supported versions. | /api/mcp server (workstream 5) | modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/versioning | Implementer (p16/verify), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| Streamable HTTP 2026-07-28: one MCP endpoint that accepts POST; each JSON-RPC request is its own POST with `Accept` listing `application/json` and `text/event-stream`; the server answers with `Content-Type: application/json` or a request scoped SSE stream. Required headers: `MCP-Protocol-Version` (must equal the body `_meta` value), `Mcp-Method`, and `Mcp-Name` for `tools/call`, `resources/read`, `prompts/get` (base64 sentinel `=?base64?...?=` when not plain ASCII). Header and body mismatch or a missing header: 400 with JSON-RPC error `-32020` HeaderMismatch. Unsupported version: 400 with UnsupportedProtocolVersionError. Unknown method: 404 with `-32601`. Invalid `Origin`: 403. Accepted notification: 202. GET or DELETE: 405; ignore `Mcp-Session-Id` and `Last-Event-ID`. Closing the SSE stream cancels the request. SSE responses should send `X-Accel-Buffering: no`. The HTTP+SSE transport of 2024-11-05 is deprecated. | apps/web/src/app/api/mcp route | modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http | Implementer (p16/verify), fetched 2026-09-29 |
| No MCP SDK is in the workspace today (no `@modelcontextprotocol` entry in any package.json or pnpm-lock.yaml). Whether the published TypeScript SDK supports 2026-07-28 was not checked; the MCP builder checks that before adding it, or implements the small POST only surface directly and also answers 2025-11-25 clients if agents still send `initialize`. | same | repository at p16/integration | Implementer (p16/verify), read 2026-09-29 |

## PHASE_16 review fix: conditional source writes for API photos (checked 2026-09-29)

| Fact used | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| R2 PutObject supports the conditional headers If-Match, If-None-Match, If-Modified-Since and If-Unmodified-Since. The AWS SDK's `PutObjectCommand` takes `IfNoneMatch` (present in the workspace's @aws-sdk/client-s3 types). A put with `If-None-Match: *` on an existing key fails its precondition, answered as S3's 412 PreconditionFailed, which the code reads as "already stored". | apps/web/src/lib/r2.ts `putSourceObjectIfAbsent`, used by lib/api-v1/photos.ts `storePackPhotos` | developers.cloudflare.com/r2/api/s3/api/ (PutObject row, fetched 2026-09-29); the R2 page does not state the failure status, so 412 is the S3 convention, also matched by error name `PreconditionFailed` | Implementer (p16/fix-other), 2026-09-29 |

Not verified: the exact status R2 returns for a failed PutObject precondition against a live bucket (no production access in this pass); a different status would surface as a 503 "could not save a photo" on retry rather than an overwrite.


**Anthropic thinking and effort for recipe calls (audit fix, 2026-09-29)**

| Fact | Where | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Sonnet 5 runs adaptive thinking when the `thinking` parameter is omitted; `{type: "disabled"}` is accepted; `budget_tokens` returns 400. Thinking tokens count toward `max_tokens` and bill as output, so a budget tuned without thinking can stop at `max_tokens` with only a thinking block, or a cut off tool call. | packages/ai anthropicLLM.ts (modelOptions, output_truncated); seed recipes intake v6, analyzer v3, planner v2 (maxTokens 8000, 8000, 16000) | platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/thinking (as quoted in the audit, checked 2026-09-29); Claude API reference bundled with Claude Code, Sonnet 5 migration notes | Audit fixer (audit/fix-pipeline), 2026-09-29 |
| Effort is `output_config: {effort}` with `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`; default `high` on Sonnet 5 and `medium` on Opus 5.5. Opus 5.5 rejects `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` at every effort; Haiku 4.5 rejects `effort`. So recipe options are keyed per model id and each adapter sends only its own model's entry; the seed sets `effort: "medium"` for Sonnet 5 and Opus 5.5 and nothing for Haiku 4.5. | same | Claude API reference bundled with Claude Code (Thinking and Effort table, cached 2026-06-24), cross checked 2026-09-29 | Audit fixer (audit/fix-pipeline), 2026-09-29 |
| Forced `tool_choice` (`{type: "tool"}`) works with thinking on Sonnet 5 on the Claude API (Bedrock alone needs thinking disabled for it). Opus 5.5 returns 400 for forced `tool_choice` `any` or `tool`, so it cannot serve as the fallback for the forced tool recipes as they stand. Fixed on audit/fix-trigger: llmJson now sends tool_choice auto with strict tools on every model (see below). | trigger/src/pipeline-runner.ts llmJson | same | Audit fixer (audit/fix-pipeline), 2026-09-29 |
| Second cutout row: the same fal `fal-ai/birefnet/v2` endpoint, parameters and budget as the primary, on a second fal account read from FAL_KEY_BACKUP. No new API shape or price; the fal rows above apply. | packages/pipeline/src/seed/models.ts cutoutModelSeedRows | fal rows above | Audit fixer (audit/fix-pipeline), 2026-09-29 |

## 2026-09-29: audit fixes in trigger (branch audit/fix-trigger)

- Anthropic forced tool use: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5) answers 400 to `tool_choice` `{"type": "tool"}` and `{"type": "any"}`; `auto` plus `strict: true` on the tool is the supported way to get schema valid arguments (platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/define-tools, forcing tool use; claude-api skill reference, checked 2026-09-29). llmJson now sends `{"type": "auto"}` on every model, tells the model to call emit_result in the tool description, and asks once more when a reply carries no tool call.
- OpenAI image generation guide (developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/image-generation, Limitations, fetched 2026-09-29): "Complex prompts may take up to 2 minutes to process." Seeded image rows now carry a per attempt timeout floor: gpt-image-2 150 s, gemini-3.1-flash-image 90 s (the bridge adds the 30 s download margin). A sync image call the router times out is metered at the image price and never retried on the same provider.
- BFL (docs.bfl.ai/flux_2/flux2_image_editing and docs.bfl.ai/quick_start/pricing, fetched 2026-09-29): FLUX.2 edits default to the input size, up to 4 MP; FLUX.2 [pro] is "from $0.03" text to image and "from $0.045" editing, "pricing varies by output resolution", with no per megapixel table published for pro (the pricing calculator at bfl.ai/pricing has it). The harmonize draft now goes to BFL at most 1440 x 1440 (about 2 MP, multiples of 16). The flat 60,000 micros per call stays; confirm it against the calculator for a 2 MP in, 2 MP out edit.

The adapter retries every 429 and 5xx that is not a billing code, so the missing code name changes nothing. | NUANCE | shared.ts `httpProviderError`, `retryAfterMsFrom`; router `retryDelayMs` | same | Docs builder (p17/docs) |

Prompt caching guide: "input tokens use the uncached-input, cached-input, or cache-write rate", and its monitoring example computes ordinary input as `inputTokens - cachedTokens - cacheWriteTokens`, so `cached_tokens` and `cache_write_tokens` are both parts of `input_tokens`. | CONFIRMED | seed `llmModelPrices.cacheWriteMicrosPerMTok`; openaiLLM.ts `costOf` and the reported `usage.inputTokens` (uncached) | developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing; /api/docs/guides/prompt-caching | Implementer (p17/fix-ai), fetched 2026-10-01 |

Not verified in this pass, because each needs a live call: the two image multipliers above, whether the production key's project allows `/v1/responses`, and real Retry-After values. They are founder steps in docs/PENDING.md, "Phase 17 founder steps". Because gpt-6-astra has no 2048 pixel box at `detail: high`, the cap estimate can be low for a very wide or very tall image on astra (the estimate shrinks it more than astra does). Astra is only the last step of the qc_judge escalation, so the gap is small; it is listed under Known gaps in PHASE_17.md.

## Site visitor count, site-visitors (checked 2026-10-01)

The cookieless visitor count (apps/web/src/lib/visits, POST /api/visits, /app/ops/visitors, migration 0027) relies on these external facts.

| Fact | Status | Where the code relies on it | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Plausible's method: "hash(daily_salt + website_domain + ip_address + user_agent)"; "The salt is rotated and deleted every 24 hours."; "Raw IP addresses and User-Agent data are never stored." The count follows it with one addition: the hash is HMAC-SHA256 under `VISITS_HASH_KEY`, a secret kept only in the server's environment, because the salts sit in the database next to terms_acceptances, which holds the IP and user agent of each signed up user, so a plain hash could be recomputed from database contents alone. The first 16 bytes are kept as hex, the parts are joined by a newline so they cannot run together, and the configured site host is the domain. Salts older than yesterday are deleted by the stale-jobs cron (every 10 to 15 minutes once scheduled), by the first page view of a new UTC day and whenever /app/ops/visitors opens, so a salt lives about two days (one more than Plausible). Copies in Supabase backups are not deleted early; without the key they cannot recompute a code. Only the UTC day is stored, no time of day. | CONFIRMED (method); the key is our addition | lib/visits/hash.ts `visitorHash`; lib/visits/key.ts; lib/visits/store.ts `saltFor`, `deleteExpiredVisitSalts`; app/api/cron/stale-jobs/route.ts | plausible.io/data-policy | Implementer (site-visitors), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Client IP on Render. None of render.com/docs/web-services, /docs/ddos-protection, /docs/inbound-ip-rules, /docs/web-service-caching or /docs/configure-cloudflare-dns says which header carries the client IP, whether Render strips client supplied values, or whether any country header (such as CF-IPCountry) reaches the service. ddos-protection says Render uses "Cloudflare's industry-leading DDoS protection infrastructure behind the scenes", and edge cached responses carry `CF-Cache-Status`, a Cloudflare header. So the count reuses `clientIp()` unchanged and stores no country. Item 2 of "Still unverified (batch 1)" stands. For the count, any client that sends a different user agent, or a forged IP header, on every request makes a new visitor each time; the bot filter, the same origin check and the per code daily cap do not stop that. Bounds added 2026-10-01: 300 counted page views and 30 new visitor codes an hour per IP (`visits.record`, `visits.newVisitor`), at most 3 beacons worked on at once per instance, and at most 100,000 stored page views a day per instance. The per IP bounds become reliable only once `clientIp()` trusts edge set headers alone; until then a forged IP header gets past them and the in flight bound and the daily ceiling are what hold. The site's own beacon never sends such a header. | UNVERIFIED (no Render page documents it) | lib/rate-limit.ts `clientIp` and the two visits policies, used by app/api/visits/route.ts and lib/visits/record.ts; lib/visits/store.ts `DAILY_PAGE_VIEW_CEILING` | render.com/docs pages listed | Implementer (site-visitors), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| isbot 5.2.2 (latest on npm, registry modified 2026-08-27), license Unlicense (public domain, no notice needed), no dependencies, Node 18 or later, ESM and CJS builds. `isbot(userAgent)` tests a user agent against the package's maintained pattern list, which also matches HeadlessChrome, curl and python-requests (unit tests in lib/visits/visits.test.ts). | CONFIRMED | lib/visits/bots.ts | npm view isbot (registry.npmjs.org); github.com/omrilotan/isbot | Implementer (site-visitors), 2026-10-01 |
| Chrome sends `Sec-Purpose: prefetch` on prefetches and `Sec-Purpose: prefetch;prerender` on prerenders, and subresource requests of a prerendering page carry it too. A page knows it is prerendering from `document.prerendering` and gets `prerenderingchange` when it is shown. MDN lists `prefetch` as the only defined value. The legacy `Purpose: prefetch`, `X-Moz: prefetch` and `X-Purpose: preview` headers are filtered as well, on general knowledge, not a page checked in this pass. | CONFIRMED (Chrome); NUANCE (legacy headers) | lib/visits/bots.ts `isPrefetch`; components/visit-beacon.tsx waits for `prerenderingchange` | developer.chrome.com/docs/web-platform/prerender-pages; developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Sec-Purpose | Implementer (site-visitors), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `navigator.sendBeacon` sends a POST asynchronously and the browser does not wait for a response; it returns true when the data was queued. For requests that need other properties, MDN points to `fetch` with `keepalive: true`, which the beacon uses as its fallback. | CONFIRMED | components/visit-beacon.tsx | developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigator/sendBeacon | Implementer (site-visitors), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `pageshow` fires on a Window when the browser navigates to a document, including "Returning to the page using the browser's forward or back buttons (including when restored from the bfcache)"; `PageTransitionEvent.persisted` "indicates if a webpage is loading from a cache", true for a back or forward cache restore. The beacon counts a `pageshow` with `persisted` true, without a referrer, since a restored page runs no React effect again. | CONFIRMED | components/visit-beacon.tsx `onPageShow` | developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/pageshow_event; /en-US/docs/Web/API/PageTransitionEvent/persisted | Implementer (site-visitors fixes), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Supabase `User.email_confirmed_at?: string` is set once the address is confirmed; the operator gate requires it as well as the email being in OPS_EMAILS (or OPS_EMAIL when OPS_EMAILS is unset). | CONFIRMED | lib/ops.ts `isOperator` | installed @supabase/auth-js 2.117.2, dist/module/lib/types.d.ts | Implementer (site-visitors), 2026-10-01 |

To close the IP row: after the deploy, send a beacon from outside with forged `cf-connecting-ip`, `true-client-ip`, `x-real-ip` and `x-forwarded-for` headers to a route that logs what arrives, as item 2 of "Still unverified (batch 1)" describes, and make `clientIp()` trust only headers the edge sets.

## PHASE_19: ChatGPT and Codex plugin (checked 2026-10-01, branch p19/integration)

Method: P19-01. Every external fact docs/phases/PHASE_19.md relies on, re-fetched on 2026-10-01 from the official page named by its key (the source table is in PHASE_19.md, "Requirements matrix"), read as text through WebFetch. The four research passes of the same day (research_notes/Curvi ChatGPT app listing/) are the first reading; this pass checks them again at build time. help.openai.com (O14) refuses direct fetches, and production (curvi.ai and the Supabase project) was not called from this branch, so O14 and the live probes (L1) are carried from the research notes and marked so. CONFIRMED means the page says what the plan says; NUANCE means it does, with a detail the plan left out or that changes how the code must do it; CONFLICT means two official pages disagree, and the row says which one the plan follows; UNVERIFIED means no page fetched in this pass says it. "Used in" names the file that relies on the fact, or the PHASE_19 item that will add it. Every later PHASE_19 lane adds its own rows below this table when it relies on a fact that is not here.

### OpenAI plugins, ChatGPT and Codex

| Fact | Status | Used in | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ChatGPT connects MCP servers with "OAuth, No Authentication, and Mixed Authentication". Neither the auth page nor developer mode lists API keys or custom headers, so ChatGPT cannot send a Curvi API key, even privately. "Supported MCP protocols: SSE and streaming HTTP." | CONFIRMED | Decision 2; lib/mcp-auth (P19-08); app/api/mcp | O1 developers.openai.com/plugins/build/auth; O9 developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/developer-mode | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Protected resource metadata with `resource`, `authorization_servers`, `scopes_supported`; a 401 carries `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="...", scope="..."`. MCP: servers MUST implement RFC 9728; SHOULD send `scope` in the challenge; clients use it first, else `scopes_supported`. | CONFIRMED | app/.well-known routes and lib/mcp-auth/challenge.ts (P19-06) | O1; M1 modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| "Your authorization server must publish code_challenge_methods_supported with S256." Clients register by CIMD, DCR or predefined OAuth clients. | CONFIRMED | Decision 1; runbook A3, B5 | O1 | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Token checks: verify the signature and `iss`, deny expired tokens, "Confirm the token was minted for your server (aud or the resource claim) and contains the scopes." ### MCP specification and registry

| Fact | Status | Used in | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Servers "SHOULD NOT include offline_access in WWW-Authenticate scope or Protected Resource Metadata scopes_supported". Clients send `resource` (RFC 8707) in the authorization and token requests, "regardless of whether authorization servers support it". Insufficient scope: 403 with `error="insufficient_scope"`, `scope`, `resource_metadata`. Authorization servers SHOULD send `iss`; clients compare a present `iss` and proceed when it is absent and not advertised. | CONFIRMED | Resource metadata (P19-06); verify.ts (P19-07) | M1 | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| 2026-07-28 requires `ttlMs` and `cacheScope` (`"public"` or `"private"`) on `tools/list`, `prompts/list`, `resources/list`, `resources/read` and `resources/templates/list`; a missing resource is now `-32602` (Invalid Params); tools SHOULD come back in a deterministic order. | CONFIRMED | lib/api-v1/mcp.ts resources seam (`-32602` for an unknown uri, this branch); ttlMs and cacheScope (P19-13) | M2 modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog; M4 | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| 2026-07-28 removes `ping`, `logging/setLevel` and the `initialize` handshake. The server keeps answering `ping` and `initialize` for 2025 clients (dual era), which the plan's "server/discover and ping stay open" assumes; a modern client will not send `ping`. | NUANCE | lib/api-v1/mcp.ts | M2 | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Streamable HTTP: servers "MUST validate the Origin header"; "If the Origin header is present and invalid, servers MUST respond with HTTP 403 Forbidden." The page does not define a valid origin, so an allowlist (P19-20) fits it. `Mcp-Name` is required on `tools/call`, `resources/read` (from `params.uri`) and `prompts/get`, base64 sentinel decoded before comparison. | CONFIRMED | lib/api-v1/mcp.ts (Mcp-Name on resources/read while resources are served, this branch); `isAllowedMcpOrigin` (P19-20) | M3 modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| MCP Apps: `ui://` resources with `text/html;profile=mcp-app`; `_meta.ui.resourceUri`; `_meta.ui.visibility` `["model"]`, `["app"]`; CSP `resourceDomains`, `connectDomains`, `frameDomains`; optional `_meta.ui.domain`; `ui/open-link`, `ui/initialize`, `ui/notifications/tool-input`, `ui/notifications/tool-result`. | CONFIRMED | `McpResourceProvider` seam (this branch); viewer (P19-19) | M5 | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| MCP Registry domain proof at `https://<domain>/.well-known/mcp-registry-auth` holding `v=MCPv1; k=ed25519; p=...`; names in reverse DNS (`com.example.*/*`, so `ai.curvi/*`); `mcp-publisher login http --domain ... --private-key ...`; "macOS's system LibreSSL does not support Ed25519 in genpkey": install OpenSSL 3, or use ECDSA P-384 keys, which LibreSSL can make. Version immutability and the `deleted` status were not re-read in this pass. | NUANCE (P-384 option) | Registry route and runbook (P19-28) | MR1 github.com/modelcontextprotocol/registry, docs/modelcontextprotocol-io/authentication.mdx | Implementer (p19/integration), fetched 2026-10-01 |

### p19/auth: sign in (P19-04 to P19-08, P19-11), checked 2026-10-01

Rows the sign in lane relies on beyond the table above, each read on 2026-10-01 from the source named. Supabase Auth source is github.com/supabase/auth, branch master.

| Fact | Status | Used in | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `AccessTokenClaims` has `client_id`, `scope` and `session_id`, each `omitempty`; for OAuth sessions `GenerateAccessToken` sets `ClientID`, `Scope` (from `session.Scopes`) and `SessionId`; `aud` is `jwt.ClaimStrings{user.Aud}` ("authenticated") until a hook changes it. `scope` is one string, not an array. | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/mcp-auth/verify.ts (client_id allowlist, session_id, scope check); migration 0028 hook | SB4 internal/tokens/service.go | Implementer (p19/auth), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Scopes are a space separated string (`ParseScopeString`); supported: `openid`, `profile`, `email`, `phone`, `offline_access`. The scope check therefore splits the claim on whitespace and needs `openid` and `email`; a missing or non string claim fails as `insufficient_scope`. P19-12 still records the claim of a real ChatGPT token. | CONFIRMED | verify.ts `scopesOf` | SB4 internal/models/oauth_scope.go | Implementer (p19/auth), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `auth.sessions` (`Session`) has `id`, `user_id`, `not_after`, `oauth_client_id` (uuid) and `scopes`; `RevokeOAuthSessions` runs `DELETE FROM sessions WHERE user_id = ? AND oauth_client_id = ?`, so a revoked grant leaves no session. The session check reads `id`, `user_id` and `not_after`. Whether the owner role may read `auth.sessions` on the hosted project is still unverified (P19-12); the code falls back to the Auth API on 42501, 42P01, 3F000 or 42703. | CONFIRMED (columns); UNVERIFIED (hosted privilege) | apps/web/src/lib/mcp-auth/sessions.ts `DbSessionChecker`; P19-10 admin revoke | SB4 internal/models/sessions.go | Implementer (p19/auth), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `requireAuthentication` loads the session from the `session_id` claim and answers 403 `session_not_found` ("Session from session_id claim in JWT does not exist") when it is gone, and 403 `user_banned` for a banned user. Error codes `session_not_found`, `session_expired`, `user_not_found`, `bad_jwt`; The JSON error body is `{ code: <HTTP status>, error_code, msg, error_id }` (`HTTPError`), so the check reads `error_code`. The fallback session check counts `session_not_found`, `session_expired`, `user_not_found` and `user_banned` as ended and anything else as unknown (fail closed with 503, never a 401 loop). | CONFIRMED | sessions.ts `AuthApiSessionChecker` (with `X-JWT-AUD`, see the `UserGet` row above) | SB4 internal/api/auth.go, internal/api/apierrors/apierrors.go; supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/debugging/error-codes | Implementer (p19/auth), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Restrictive policy pattern `create policy ... as restrictive for update to authenticated using ((select auth.jwt()->>'aal') = 'aal2')`; wrapping `auth.jwt()` in `select` lets Postgres run it once per statement (initPlan). `no_oauth_clients` uses `((select auth.jwt()) ->> 'client_id') IS NULL` for USING and WITH CHECK, `AS RESTRICTIVE FOR ALL TO authenticated`, on every public table. | CONFIRMED | Migration 0028; packages/db/src/mcp-connections.test.ts | supabase.com/docs/guides/database/postgres/row-level-security; SB1 token security (row above) | Implementer (p19/auth), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `jose` 6.2.12 added as a direct dependency of apps/web (`^6.2.12`). `jwtVerify(jwt, key, options)` takes `algorithms`, `audience`, `issuer`, `clockTolerance`, `requiredClaims`, `currentDate`; `createRemoteJWKSet(url, options)` defaults: `cacheMaxAge` 600000 ms, `cooldownDuration` 30000 ms (a missing kid refetches at most that often), `timeoutDuration` 5000 ms. Error classes read from the installed package: `JWTExpired`, `JWTClaimValidationFailed` (with `claim`), `JOSEAlgNotAllowed`, `JWKSNoMatchingKey`, `JWKSTimeout`, `JWKSInvalid`; a failed key set fetch throws the generic `JOSEError`, which verify.ts answers as unavailable (503), not as a bad token. | CONFIRMED | verify.ts | github.com/panva/jose docs/jwt/verify/functions/jwtVerify.md, docs/jwks/remote/interfaces/RemoteJWKSetOptions.md; node_modules/jose/dist/webapi | Implementer (p19/auth), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Next.js 15.5.26 (the installed version) finds app routes with `recursiveReadDir(appDir, { ignorePartFilter: part => part.startsWith('_') })`, so a folder starting with a dot is routed and `app/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/route.ts` serves `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` with no rewrite. Read in the package source, not a doc; P19-12 step 1 curls both paths on production. | NUANCE (source, not docs) | apps/web/src/app/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource (both routes) | node_modules/next/dist/build/entries.js:136-138, dist/server/dev/next-dev-server.js:228 | Implementer (p19/auth), 2026-10-01 |
| Hook implementation choice: `curvi_access_token_hook` sets `claims.aud` only when `claims.client_id` is a non empty JSON string, and returns every other event unchanged (including `client_id` null, a number or an empty string), so a web session never gets the MCP audience; `jsonb_set(..., create_missing => true)` adds `aud` if a claims object lacks it. Runs as `language sql stable` with `search_path = ''`, reads no table and cannot raise on any JSON input (packages/db tests). | CONFIRMED (tests) | Migration 0028 | SB2 (row above); packages/db/src/mcp-connections.test.ts | Implementer (p19/auth), 2026-10-01 |
