The consented path reads `client_id`, `scope`, `redirect_uri`, `status` and `approved_at` by `authorization_id` and `user_id`, joined to `client_name`. Whether the owner role may read these tables and delete from `auth.sessions` on the hosted project is not verified (no production call); P19-12 checks it, and both reads fail closed meanwhile. | CONFIRMED (columns); UNVERIFIED (hosted privilege) | apps/web/src/lib/mcp-auth/consent-backend.ts `storedAuthorization`, `endSessions` | SB4 migrations/20250804100000_add_oauth_authorizations_consents.up.sql, 20250731150234_add_oauth_clients_table.up.sql | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `AuthorizationID` is `crypto.SecureAlphanumeric(32)`; the consent page URL carries it as `authorization_id`. GET `/oauth/authorizations/{id}` loads it for update, answers 404 "authorization not found" when missing or expired and 400 "authorization request cannot be processed" when it is no longer pending, binds the user on the first GET, and with an existing consent covering the scopes approves at once and answers only `redirect_url`. The consent POST needs a pending request of the same user. So a second GET of an auto approved request fails, and the consented path keeps the link the page received; a 4xx reads as expired. | CONFIRMED | consent-backend.ts `failureOf`, `parseAuthorizationId`; consent.ts consented path | SB4 internal/api/oauthserver/authorize.go, internal/models/oauth_authorization.go | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| auth-js builds the request path as `` `${this.url}/oauth/authorizations/${authorizationId}` `` with no escaping, so the page accepts only `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}` before calling it (a crafted id could otherwise reach another Auth API path with the user's session). `getAuthorizationDetails` answers `OAuthAuthorizationDetails` (`authorization_id`, `redirect_uri`, `client { id, name, uri, logo_uri }`, `user { id, email }`, `scope` as a space separated string) or `OAuthRedirect` (`redirect_url`); `approveAuthorization` and `denyAuthorization` take `{ skipBrowserRedirect }` and answer `redirect_url`; `revokeGrant({ clientId })`; every method answers `AuthSessionMissingError` without a session. | CONFIRMED (installed package) | consent-backend.ts `DbConsentBackend` | node_modules/@supabase/auth-js 2.117.2 dist/module/GoTrueClient.js (5261 to 5381), lib/types.d.ts (2523 to 2715) | Implementer (p19/consent), 2026-10-01 |
| DELETE `/user/oauth/grants?client_id=...` (auth-js `revokeGrant`) revokes the consent and runs `RevokeOAuthSessions` (deletes the user's sessions for that client, so their refresh tokens stop), answering 204. The admin disconnect of a member's connection, which has no session of that member, deletes `auth.sessions` where `user_id` and `oauth_client_id` match instead, the same statement `RevokeOAuthSessions` runs (row above, p19/auth). | CONFIRMED | lib/mcp-auth/connected-apps.ts `disconnectConnectedApp` | SB4 internal/api/oauthserver/handlers.go (`UserRevokeOAuthGrant`), internal/models/sessions.go | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Logout scopes: global (auth-js's default for `signOut()`) runs `models.Logout`, which is `DELETE FROM sessions WHERE user_id = ?`, so it also ends every OAuth session of the user, ChatGPT's included; local deletes only the current session; others deletes all but the current one. "Use another account" on the consent page therefore signs out with `scope: "local"`. New finding: the web Sign out (`app/auth/signout/route.ts`) and the account deletion action call `signOut()` with the default global scope, so signing out of curvi.ai on any device ends the user's ChatGPT sessions too; ChatGPT then has to sign in again (silently while a live connection exists). Left unchanged here; a founder decision for the integrator (docs/LAUNCH_CHECKLIST.md, p19/consent). | CONFIRMED (new finding) | consent-backend.ts `signOutHere`; app/oauth/consent/actions.ts `switchAccountAction` | SB4 internal/api/logout.go, internal/models/sessions.go; node_modules/@supabase/auth-js GoTrueClient.js `signOut(options = { scope: 'global' })` | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Supabase's consent page pattern: "Your page checks if the user is signed in, redirecting to sign in if not (preserving the `authorization_id`)"; the decision methods return a `redirect_url` the page sends the user to. The authorization path is joined to the Site URL. Listing and revoking grants are not on that page (the source rows above cover them). | CONFIRMED | app/oauth/consent (sign in inline, back to the same URL) | SB1 supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/oauth-server/getting-started | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `redirect()` "accepts absolute URLs and can be used to redirect to external links"; in a Server Action it performs a client side navigation when JavaScript is available and a 303 for a progressive enhancement submission; it throws, so it is called outside `try`. The page is Next.js 15.5 and the doc page is the current one, so the e2e run (Connect lands on the stubbed chatgpt.com callback) is the check. | CONFIRMED (e2e) | app/oauth/consent/actions.ts, page.tsx | nextjs.org/docs/app/api-reference/functions/redirect; e2e/oauth-consent.spec.ts | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| CSP `form-action` and redirects: "Whether form-action should block redirects after a form submission is debated ... (e.g., Firefox 57 doesn't block the redirects whereas Chrome 63 does)." The consent action's 303 to ChatGPT (no JavaScript) is a redirect after a form post, so `https://chatgpt.com` joins `form-action` (the policy is report only today). | CONFIRMED | apps/web/next.config.ts | developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/form-action | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| CORS: a preflight is answered with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, `-Methods`, `-Headers` and `-Max-Age`; a server that names one origin sends `Vary: Origin`; only CORS safelisted response headers are readable unless listed in `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` (so `WWW-Authenticate` is exposed for the sign in challenge); the actual response also needs `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. The MCP allowlist itself rests on the M3 row above (Origin "MUST" be validated, 403 when invalid, no definition of valid). | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/api-v1/mcp-cors.ts; app/api/mcp/route.ts `OPTIONS` | developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/CORS; M3 (row above) | Implementer (p19/consent), fetched 2026-10-01 |

### p19/ui: pack viewer (P19-19), checked 2026-10-01, branch p19/ui

Method: the MCP Apps specification and the SDK types fetched from github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps (main) on 2026-10-01 (`specification/2026-01-26/apps.mdx` and `src/spec.types.ts`; the `specification` folder holds `2026-01-26` and `draft`, so 2026-01-26 is the current stable version), and OpenAI's plugin reference (O2), "Build your ChatGPT UI" and the UI guidelines (O11), security and privacy (O12) and submission errors (O4) re-read through WebFetch the same day. Every fact the viewer code relies on is below; rows above that already hold (O2 `openExternal`, O2 and M5 visibility, O11 UI, M5 MCP Apps) are not repeated.

| Fact | Status | Used in | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| UI resource: the uri MUST start with `ui://`; `mimeType` MUST be `text/html;profile=mcp-app` ("other types reserved for future extensions"); content in `text` (or base64 `blob`); "Content MUST be valid HTML5 document". OpenAI: "`text/html;profile=mcp-app` Standard profile for MCP App components". | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/mcp-ui/pack-viewer/resource.ts (`PACK_VIEWER_URI` `ui://curvi/pack-viewer/v1.html`, `MCP_APP_MIME_TYPE`), html.ts | M5 apps.mdx "UI Resource Format"; O2 developers.openai.com/plugins/reference | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Resource `_meta.ui`: `csp` (`connectDomains`, `resourceDomains`, `frameDomains`, `baseUriDomains`), `permissions`, `domain`, `prefersBorder`; declared on the resources/list entry and on each resources/read content item. "Empty or omitted = no external connections (secure default)"; with no `ui.csp` the host MUST use `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; media-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'none';`, so the previews need `resourceDomains`. Hosts "MUST NOT allow undeclared domains". OpenAI places `_meta.ui.csp` and `_meta.ui.domain` in "Resource contents", and "The plugin review process checks the declared policy against the UI behavior." | CONFIRMED | resource.ts `packViewerMeta` (both places; `resourceDomains` the site origin, no connect or frame domains) | M5 apps.mdx; O2; O11 developers.openai.com/plugins/build/chatgpt-ui | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| `_meta.ui.domain`: OpenAI: "Dedicated origin for hosted components (required when submitting a plugin with UI; must be unique per plugin). Defaults to `https://web-sandbox.oaiusercontent.com`"; its UI page example sets `domain: "https://example.com"` (the developer's own origin). MCP Apps: "Host-dependent ... Servers MUST consult host-specific documentation", naming "URL-derived subdomains (e.g., `www-example-com.oaiusercontent.com`)" as the OpenAI pattern. Curvi sends its site origin (`https://curvi.ai` in production), which is unique to it; the exact value ChatGPT accepts is confirmed at the first developer mode run with the viewer ("Still unverified" in PHASE_19.md). | NUANCE (value from the example, not a rule) | resource.ts `packViewerMeta` | O2; O11; M5 apps.mdx `UIResourceMeta.domain` | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Legacy keys: `_meta["openai/widgetCSP"]` is the "Legacy ChatGPT compatibility key for widget CSP metadata" with `connect_domains`, `resource_domains`, `frame_domains` and `redirect_domains` (the targets `window.openai.openExternal` may open); `openai/widgetDomain` and `openai/widgetPrefersBorder` are aliases of `ui.domain` and `ui.prefersBorder`; `openai/outputTemplate` is an "optional/compatibility alias" of `_meta.ui.resourceUri`; `openai/widgetAccessible` (default false) is a compatibility field, "prefer `_meta.ui.visibility`". Curvi sends only `openai/widgetCSP` (for `redirect_domains`, which `ui.csp` lacks) beside the standard keys. | CONFIRMED | resource.ts; mcp-tools.ts (no `openai/outputTemplate`, no `openai/widgetAccessible`) | O2 | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Tool `_meta.ui.resourceUri` names the template (the flat `_meta["ui/resourceUri"]` "is deprecated ... will be removed before GA"); OpenAI: "Attach only to render tools, not data-fetching tools" and "Treat the resource URI as a cache key. When you make a breaking change ... publish a new URI". `visibility` defaults to `["model", "app"]`; the host MUST reject an app's `tools/call` for a tool without `"app"`. | CONFIRMED | mcp-tools.ts (`resourceUri` on create_pack only; get_pack is the one tool with `"app"`); the `v1` in the uri | M5 apps.mdx "Resource Discovery"; O11 | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Servers "SHOULD check client capabilities before registering UI-enabled tools" (the `io.modelcontextprotocol/ui` extension with `mimeTypes`), "SHOULD provide text-only fallback behavior for all UI-enabled tools", and a host without MCP Apps treats the tool "as standard tool (text-only fallback)". Curvi's server is stateless (tools/list never sees the initialize capabilities), so `resourceUri` goes to every client; every result still carries its links and status as text (R36), which is the fallback. | NUANCE (no per client check) | mcp-tools.ts; app/api/mcp/mcp-viewer.test.ts (links in the text block) | M5 apps.mdx "Client<>Server Capability Negotiation", "Resource Discovery" | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Bridge: JSON-RPC 2.0 over `postMessage` with the parent ("UI iframes act as MCP clients"). The view sends `ui/initialize`, then `ui/notifications/initialized`; "The Host MUST NOT send any request or notification to the View before it receives an initialized notification." The SDK's `McpUiInitializeRequest.params` is `{ appInfo, appCapabilities, protocolVersion }` and the spec text says the view "MUST include its capabilities in the `appCapabilities` field", while the spec's transport example still sends `{ capabilities, clientInfo, protocolVersion }`. The viewer sends the SDK shape with `protocolVersion` "2026-01-26" (`LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION`). The result holds `protocolVersion`, `hostInfo`, `hostCapabilities` and `hostContext` (`theme`, `styles.variables`, `displayMode`, `availableDisplayModes`, `containerDimensions` and more). | CONFLICT (example against SDK; SDK followed) | runtime.ts `startPackViewer`; html.ts `MCP_APPS_PROTOCOL_VERSION` | M5 apps.mdx "Transport Layer", "App Capabilities in ui/initialize", "Host Context"; ext-apps src/spec.types.ts | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Host to view: `ui/notifications/tool-input` (`{ arguments }`, "sent at most once and is required before sending `ui/notifications/tool-result`"), `ui/notifications/tool-input-partial`, `ui/notifications/tool-result` (a `CallToolResult`), `ui/notifications/tool-cancelled` (`{ reason? }`), `ui/notifications/host-context-changed` (partial host context, `displayMode` included), and the request `ui/resource-teardown`, which the view answers with `{}` ("Host SHOULD wait for a response before tearing down"). | CONFIRMED | runtime.ts message handler; state.ts (awaiting approval until tool-input; cancelled) | M5 apps.mdx; spec.types.ts | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| View to host: `tools/call` (proxied when `hostCapabilities.serverTools` is present; ChatGPT alias `window.openai.callTool(name, args)`); `ui/open-link` `{ url }`, answered `{}` or a JSON-RPC error such as `-32000` "Link opening denied by user" (`hostCapabilities.openLinks`; the SDK result may carry `isError`); `ui/request-display-mode` `{ mode }`, answered with the mode actually set (the view MUST declare its modes in `appCapabilities.availableDisplayModes` and check the host's `availableDisplayModes` first); `ui/notifications/size-changed` `{ width?, height? }` ("The View SHOULD send this notification when rendered content body size changes"). `ui/download-file` (`hostCapabilities.downloadFile`) is in the SDK's main branch but not in the 2026-01-26 spec, so it is not used. | CONFIRMED (download-file not in the spec) | runtime.ts (`poll`, `actions.open`, `actions.seeAll`, `reportSize`) | M5 apps.mdx "Requests", "Display Modes", "Container Dimensions"; spec.types.ts | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| ChatGPT's `window.openai`: `toolInput`, `toolOutput` (the tool's `structuredContent`), `callTool(name, args)`, `requestDisplayMode`, `setOpenInAppUrl({ href })` ("Optionally override the external target shown in fullscreen"), `openExternal({ href, redirectUrl })`, `notifyIntrinsicHeight`. OpenAI calls `ui/notifications/tool-input`, `tool-result` and `tools/call` the "portable MCP Apps mechanism" and `window.openai` the "ChatGPT compatibility aliases". The viewer uses the bridge first and the aliases only where the bridge lacks a step (no answer to `ui/initialize`, no `serverTools`, no `openLinks`), plus `setOpenInAppUrl` when present. `notifyIntrinsicHeight`'s arguments are not documented, so it is not called. | NUANCE | runtime.ts | O2; O11 | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| UI guidelines: inline card "up to two actions maximum", "No nested scrolling"; carousel "Keep to 3–8 items per carousel", "Items should always include an image", "Avoid showing more than two lines of text", "Each card may have a single, optional CTA"; "Do not include your logo as part of the response"; "Use system colors"; "Always inherit the system font stack"; WCAG AA contrast; "Provide alt text for all images". | CONFIRMED | state.ts `viewerViewOf` (card for 1 or 2, carousel for 3 to 8, See all), render.ts (title and one meta line, one Download per item, alt text), html.ts (system fonts, host colors with fallbacks, no logo) | O11 developers.openai.com/plugins/concepts/ui-guidelines | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Security: "Widgets run inside an isolated iframe with a strict Content Security Policy" and "cannot access privileged browser APIs such as `window.alert`, `window.prompt`, `window.confirm`, or `navigator.clipboard`"; "Render from `toolResult?.structuredContent`, and treat it as untrusted input"; "avoid embedding secrets or tokens in component props". | CONFIRMED | render.ts (textContent only), state.ts (links kept only on the site origin under the link routes), runtime.ts (messages only from the parent; no alert, confirm or clipboard) | O12 developers.openai.com/plugins/guides/security-privacy; O11 | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Theming variables a host may pass in `hostContext.styles.variables` include `--color-background-primary`, `--color-background-secondary`, `--color-text-primary`, `--color-text-secondary`, `--color-text-danger`, `--color-border-primary`, `--font-sans`, `--font-weight-*`, `--font-text-*-size`, `--font-heading-*-size` and `--border-radius-*`. | CONFIRMED | html.ts styles (each with a fallback); runtime.ts `applyContext` (keeps plain values only) | M5 apps.mdx "Theming" | Implementer (p19/ui), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Build check, not a doc: the viewer script is assembled from each function's `toString()`. Compiled the way Next 15.5.26's server build does (SWC with the node target, then the SWC minifier with mangling), the functions stay self contained and the minified script (about 12.8 KB) runs the same flow in the test host; the committed tests run the unminified script (about 26 KB of HTML). | CONFIRMED (one off run, not a committed test) | html.ts; runtime.test.ts | node_modules/next/dist/build/swc (options.js `getLoaderSWCOptions`: server `env.targets.node`) | Implementer (p19/ui), 2026-10-01 |

### p19/integration final gate: review fixes and P19-29, checked 2026-10-01

Method: O6 (https://developers.openai.com/plugins/plugin-guidelines) and the Node.js crypto reference (https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html) fetched on 2026-10-01 through WebFetch and read for the exact list and API shapes the code encodes. No provider was called; the live intake eval for version 8 is a founder step.

The code's ids (`registration_completed:<user id>`) are stable, so a repeat is counted once. | CONFIRMED | ads-conversions.ts (one event per request, stable ids) | developers.openai.com/ads/conversions-api.md; developers.openai.com/ads/conversion-tracking.md | Implementer (p18/measure), fetched 2026-10-01 |

## Phase 18 resilience, p18/resilience (checked 2026-10-01)

P18-03 (acquisition gate, fal balance probe, low balance alerts) and P18-23 (requeue packs a deploy interrupted) rely on these facts.

| Fact | Status | Where the code relies on it | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fal account balance: `GET https://api.fal.ai/v1/account/billing`, optional query `expand` ("Use 'credits' to include current credit balance"), header `Authorization: Key YOUR_ADMIN_API_KEY` with an Admin API key (not the inference key). 200 body `{ "username": string, "credits": { "current_balance": number (at least 0), "currency": string } }`, example `{ "username": "my-team", "credits": { "current_balance": 24.5, "currency": "USD" } }`. Errors 400, 401, 403, 429 and 500 carry an `error` object with `type`, `message`, `docs_url` and `request_id`. | CONFIRMED (docs); one live call with a real admin key is a founder step | packages/ai/src/probe.ts `probeFalBalance`, `FAL_BILLING_URL`, `parseFalBilling`; trigger/src/provider-balance.ts | fal.ai/docs/platform-apis/v1/account/billing.md | Implementer (p18/resilience), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| A campaign pause through an OpenAI Ads API. No campaign management API is documented in the research notes (research_notes/Curvi low cost marketing plan/saas_paid_advertising_2026.md lists the Ads Manager UI, a ChatGPT Ads Manager plug in, the Pixel and the Conversions API only), and help.openai.com answered 403 to the research fetches. Treated as absent: the fal pause email and the acquisition paused email say "If a ChatGPT ads campaign is running, pause it in Ads Manager. Curvi cannot pause it for you." | UNVERIFIED (no API found; manual step used) | trigger/src/provider-balance.ts `PAUSE_ADS_LINE` | research notes above; no official page reachable in this pass | Implementer (p18/resilience), 2026-10-01 |
| `next start` on SIGTERM and SIGINT (unless `NEXT_MANUAL_SIG_HANDLE` is set): closes the HTTP server and waits for pending requests, then awaits `nextServer.close()`, which waits for pending `after()` work, then calls `process.exit(0)`. So work started outside a request (no `after()`) is cut off at exit. The restart pickup therefore queues every picked up pack inside a request's `after()` (the health poll, the stale-jobs cron, a new pack's request), never from a bare timer. | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/jobs/enqueue.ts `scheduleRestartPickup`; apps/web/src/lib/jobs/restart.ts | installed next 15.5.26, node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/start-server.js (the `cleanup` handler) | Implementer (p18/resilience), 2026-10-01 |

Not verified in this pass: a live balance call with a real admin key (docs/PENDING.md, "Phase 18 founder steps, Lane 2 Resilience", step 1), and whether fal's balance can go below zero (the parser accepts any finite number).

## Phase 18 activation, p18/activation (checked 2026-10-01)

Lane 8 of docs/phases/PHASE_18.md: Google sign in (P18-13), the first run questions (P18-20) and the free white main image before signup (P18-12, shipped switched off).

| Fact | Status | Where the code relies on it | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Supabase Google provider setup: the Client ID and Client Secret go on the Google provider page of the Supabase dashboard (Authentication, Sign In / Providers, Google). In Google Cloud, "Authorized JavaScript origins" is the site's origin and "Authorized redirect URIs" is the Supabase project's callback URL, shown on that provider page (`https://<project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`). The consent screen needs the scopes `openid` (added by hand), `.../auth/userinfo.email` and `.../auth/userinfo.profile`. The call is `supabase.auth.signInWithOAuth({ provider: 'google', options: { redirectTo } })`; with PKCE (server side auth) the callback exchanges the code with `exchangeCodeForSession(code)`, which /auth/callback already does. "This redirect URL should be added to your redirect allow list." | CONFIRMED | lib/google-sign-in.ts `googleOAuthCredentials`; components/marketing/google-sign-in-button.tsx; app/auth/callback/route.ts; docs/PENDING.md "Phase 18 activation founder steps" | supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/social-login/auth-google | Implementer (p18/activation), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Redirect URL allow list: `*` matches non separator characters, `**` any characters (separators are `.` and `/`); the Site URL is the default redirect. In the auth server source, `IsRedirectURLValid` strips only the fragment before matching the allow list (the query string is part of the match), and accepts any URL with the same scheme, host and port as the Site URL without an allow list entry. So with Site URL `https://curvi.ai`, `https://curvi.ai/auth/callback?next=...&attr=...` is accepted; an allow list entry needs `**` to match the query string. | CONFIRMED | lib/signup-callback.ts `signupCallbackUrl` (query parameters on the callback URL); founder step 2 | supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/redirect-urls; github.com/supabase/auth internal/utilities/request.go (master) | Implementer (p18/activation), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Google accounts arrive confirmed. The auth server's `createAccountFromExternalIdentity` sets `EmailVerified` from the provider and calls `user.Confirm(tx)` when "decision.CandidateEmail.Verified \|\| config.Mailer.Autoconfirm"; `NewUser` leaves `EmailConfirmedAt` unset and `Confirm` writes it with `UpdateOnly(u, "confirmation_token", "email_confirmed_at")`. So a new Google user is inserted unconfirmed and confirmed by an UPDATE in the same request, which fires 0012's `on_auth_user_email_confirmed` trigger once, and the 15 credit grant pays at once. A returning user is not confirmed again. The callback sees a fresh `email_confirmed_at` and sends the new account to /welcome. | CONFIRMED (source read; no live Google sign in yet) | migration 0012 triggers; lib/verification.ts `isFreshVerification`; packages/db/src/provisioning.test.ts "pays a Google sign in once" | github.com/supabase/auth internal/api/external.go and internal/models/user.go (master) | Implementer (p18/activation), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Identity linking: "Supabase Auth automatically links identities with the same email address to a single user", never to a user with an unverified email ("pre-account takeover"), and removes unconfirmed identities of an existing user when a verified one signs in. A password account that then uses Google keeps its first provider. | CONFIRMED | lib/signup-callback.ts `signupMethodOf` (reads `app_metadata.provider`) | supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-identity-linking | Implementer (p18/activation), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Installed @supabase/auth-js 2.117.2: `SignInWithOAuthCredentials` takes `provider` and `options` (`redirectTo`, `scopes`, `queryParams`, `skipBrowserRedirect`); `OAuthResponse` is `{ data: { provider, url }, error }`; `UserAppMetadata.provider` is "The first provider that the user used to sign up with", and `app_metadata` can only be changed by the service role, so a user cannot make an email account read as Google. | CONFIRMED | lib/google-sign-in.ts; lib/signup-callback.ts `signupMethodOf` | node_modules/@supabase/auth-js/dist/module/lib/types.d.ts | Implementer (p18/activation), 2026-10-01 |
| Cloudflare R2 object lifecycle rules: dashboard path R2 object storage, the bucket, Settings, "Object Lifecycle Rules", "Add rule"; "you can specify which prefix you would like it to apply to"; an expiration after a number of days deletes objects; "Objects will typically be removed from a bucket within 24 hours of the x-amz-expiration value" (may take longer for large buckets); at most 1000 rules. So a rule on the prefix `anon/` with 2 days removes free preview files about 2 to 3 days after upload; the free_previews row expires at 2 days (`freePreview.retentionDays`), so a file can outlive its row by up to a day but is never served after the row expires (the full size route and the claim check `expires_at`). | CONFIRMED | lib/free-preview/service.ts (keys under `anon/preview/{id}/`); founder step 10 in docs/PENDING.md | developers.cloudflare.com/r2/buckets/object-lifecycles/ | Implementer (p18/activation), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| The free preview adds no new provider shape: intake runs the existing intake recipe through `llmJson` and the cutout the existing `FalCutoutProvider` through `callWithFailover` (both verified in the Phase 14 and Phase 17 rows above). With no `workspaceId` on the cutout request the R2 cutout cache wrapper is skipped (`cacheCutouts` returns the provider call unchanged when `req.workspaceId` is empty, trigger/src/cutout-cache.ts), and the claim writes the provider's cutout to the key `LiveShotGenerator.readCachedCutout` and `hasFreshUploadCutout` read (`cutoutCacheKey(workspace, upright working bytes, "png")`), which trigger/src/free-preview.test.ts checks against `hasFreshUploadCutout`. | CONFIRMED (code read) | trigger/src/free-preview.ts `runFreePreview`, `claimedCutoutCacheKey` | repository code | Implementer (p18/activation), 2026-10-01 |
| Upstash Redis price and limits for the shared per IP preview cap. | UNVERIFIED (founder step: read upstash.com/pricing on the day the database is created, PHASE_18 founder step 18) | lib/rate-limit.ts `preview.create`; lib/free-preview/gate.ts `previewSetupGaps` refuses to open without `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL` and `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` | upstash.com/pricing | Not read in this pass |

## Phase 18 email, p18/email (checked 2026-10-01)

Lane 3 of docs/phases/PHASE_18.md: lifecycle email foundation (P18-06) and the lifecycle emails (P18-07), over Resend directly (founder decision 3). Method: each official page below was fetched on 2026-10-01 and read as text. No API call was made, no email was sent and no provider money was spent.

| Fact | Status | Where the code relies on it | Source | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Resend send: `POST https://api.resend.com/emails`, `Authorization: Bearer re_...`, body `from` (friendly name allowed), `to` (string or array, at most 50), `subject`, optional `html`, `text`, `reply_to` (string or array), `headers` ("Custom email headers", an object), `tags`, `scheduled_at`; header `Idempotency-Key` (unique per request, at most 256 characters, "expires after 24 hours"); success `{ "id": ... }`. The code sends `to` as a one address array, `reply_to` as LIFECYCLE_REPLY_TO, the List-Unsubscribe headers in `headers` on marketing mail, and the dedupe key as `Idempotency-Key`, so a retry after a crash never delivers twice. `tags` is not sent (its item shape was not read). | CONFIRMED | packages/email/src/resend.ts `postResendEmail` | resend.com/docs/api-reference/emails/send-email | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Resend rate limit and request rules: "10 requests per second per team" by default (across every API key), `429` when exceeded; every request needs a `User-Agent` header (a missing one answers `403`). The code sends `User-Agent: curvi-lifecycle-email/1`, sends one email at a time with a seeded 150 ms pause, and treats 429 and 5xx as retryable. | CONFIRMED | resend.ts `RESEND_USER_AGENT`; growth.ts `emailLimits.sendSpacingMs` | resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Resend free plan quota: "daily email quota of 100 emails/day and 3,000 emails/month"; "Multiple To, CC, or BCC recipients in sent emails count as separate emails"; sent and received (inbound) emails both count. The page does not say what status a send past the quota gets. The same account carries Supabase auth mail (custom SMTP, LAUNCH_CHECKLIST step 3) and the founder alerts, so lifecycle mail is capped at 60 a day and 25 per cron run (seed `emailLimits`), 1,860 in a 31 day month. Resend Pro is $20 a month (row above, Phase 0 stack prices) if volume outgrows it. | CONFIRMED (limits); OPEN (the over quota answer, treated like any non 2xx: failed, retried if 429 or 5xx) | growth.ts `emailLimits`; packages/email/src/run.ts daily cap | resend.com/docs/knowledge-base/account-quotas-and-limits | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |

The code's GET answers 303 to the confirm page and its POST answers 200 with no redirect. | CONFIRMED | packages/email/src/render.ts `renderEmail` (headers); apps/web/src/app/api/email/unsubscribe/route.ts | resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/add-unsubscribe-to-transactional-emails | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| RFC 8058 one click unsubscribe: `List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click`; "The List-Unsubscribe header field MUST contain one HTTPS URI" (a mailto may sit beside it); the POST body "SHOULD be sent as multipart/form-data or MAY be sent as application/x-www-form-urlencoded"; the POST "MUST NOT include cookies, HTTP authorization, or any other context information", so the URI itself identifies the recipient; the sender "MUST NOT return an HTTPS redirect"; both headers "MUST be covered by the signature" of a valid DKIM-Signature (Resend signs with the domain's DKIM key; LAUNCH_CHECKLIST step 2). The token in the URI is the HMAC signed recipient key; marketing mail is held while NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL is not https. | CONFIRMED | render.ts; send.ts `unsubscribeLinks`; config.ts `marketingGaps`; app/api/email/unsubscribe/route.ts | rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8058.html | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Gmail sender guidelines: all senders need SPF or DKIM; bulk senders (5,000 a day and up) need SPF, DKIM and DMARC; keep the Postmaster Tools spam rate "below 0.10%" and "avoid ever reaching a spam rate of 0.30% or higher"; bulk senders' "Marketing messages and subscribed messages must support one-click unsubscribe, and include a clearly visible unsubscribe link in the message body" (RFC 8058). The page read did not state a two day processing rule (the research note's two days is vendor guidance; Resend's page says 48 hours; the code applies an unsubscribe at once). Curvi is far below the bulk line but follows the bulk rules anyway. | CONFIRMED | every marketing email: one click headers and the footer link (render.ts `footerLines`) | support.google.com/a/answer/81126 | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| CAN-SPAM (FTC compliance guide): "disclose clearly and conspicuously that your message is an advertisement" (wide leeway in how); "include your valid physical postal address", which may be a registered PO box or a private mailbox at a commercial mail receiving agency; a clear opt out, honored "within 10 business days"; no fee, no data beyond an email address and no step beyond a reply or "visiting a single page on an Internet website"; transactional or relationship messages are exempt from most requirements. So marketing mail starts its footer with "This is a marketing email from Curvi.", is held until CURVI_POSTAL_ADDRESS holds a real address (founder decision 4), and unsubscribes in one click or one page; transactional mail (welcome, pack ready, packs back to an account) goes without the address. Corrected in the review on 2026-10-01: lead_results (a link back to the tool, no results) and packs back to a lead are marketing, so they carry the footer and honor a marketing unsubscribe, and lead_results needs the consent box (row in "Phase 18 review fixes" below). | CONFIRMED | packages/email/src/copy.ts `MARKETING_NOTICE`; config.ts `usablePostalAddress`, `marketingGaps` | ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/can-spam-act-compliance-guide-business | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Resend webhook signatures: headers `svix-id`, `svix-timestamp`, `svix-signature` (example `v1,g0hM9SsE+OTPJTGt/tmIKtSyZlE3uFJELVlNIOLJ1OE=`), verified the Svix way: signed content `${svix_id}.${svix_timestamp}.${body}` over the raw, unaltered body; HMAC SHA-256 keyed with the base64 decoded part of the `whsec_...` secret; the header is a space separated list of `v1,<signature>` entries; compare in constant time; check the timestamp against the clock within a tolerance (the code allows 5 minutes). | CONFIRMED | packages/email/src/resend.ts `verifyResendWebhook`; app/api/webhooks/resend/route.ts | resend.com/docs/dashboard/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests; docs.svix.com/receiving/verifying-payloads/how-manual | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Resend email event types and payloads: `email.bounced` ("the recipient's mail server permanently rejected the email"; payload `data.to` an array, `data.bounce` with `message`, `subType`, `type` e.g. `Permanent`), `email.complained` (marked as spam; `data.to` array), `email.suppressed` (Resend's own suppression list), plus `email.sent`, `email.delivered`, `email.delivery_delayed`, `email.failed`, `email.opened`, `email.clicked` and others. The webhook suppresses all mail for each `data.to` address on a bounce (unless `bounce.type` is `Transient`), a complaint or a Resend suppression, and ignores the rest. | CONFIRMED | resend.ts `parseResendEvent`, `suppressionForEvent` | resend.com/docs/dashboard/webhooks/event-types; resend.com/docs/webhooks/emails/bounced; resend.com/docs/webhooks/emails/complained | Implementer (p18/email), fetched 2026-10-01 |

## Phase 18 review fixes, p18/integration (checked 2026-10-01)

| Fact | Status | Where it is used | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Stripe list charges: `GET /v1/charges` with `customer` ("Only return charges for the customer specified by this customer ID") and `limit` "between 1 and 100, and the default is 10"; newest first; "If you provide a non-existent customer ID, this call raises an error". Each charge carries `payment_method_details.card.fingerprint` (string, nullable): "Uniquely identifies this particular card number. You can use this attribute to check whether two customers who've signed up with you are using the same card number"; for Apple Pay and Google Pay "the tokenized number might be provided instead of the underlying card number". `payment_method_details.us_bank_account.fingerprint` exists too; a `link` payment's details carry no fingerprint. The installed SDK (stripe 18.5.0, API 2025-08-27.basil) types `ChargeListParams.customer` and `Charge.PaymentMethodDetails.Card.fingerprint` the same way. So the referral check (P18-24) compares the card and bank fingerprints on the referred customer's charges with the referrer customer's newest 100, treats a missing customer as no charges, and rejects a match as `same_card`. Known gap: a Link or wallet payment can carry a different or no fingerprint. | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/referrals/stripe.ts `stripeCustomerFingerprints`, `chargeFingerprints`, `referralSharesPaymentMethod` | docs.stripe.com/api/charges/list; docs.stripe.com/api/charges/object (payment_method_details.card.fingerprint) | Integrator (p18/integration review), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| Supabase auth error redirects under PKCE: `prepErrorRedirectURL` in the auth server sets `u.RawQuery = q.Encode()` (error, error_code, error_description in the query string) when `flowType == models.PKCEFlow`, and also writes them to the fragment. This app exchanges `?code=`, so an expired or used email confirmation or reset link reaches /auth/callback as `?error=...` with no code, like a refused Google sign in. The callback therefore shows the Google message only when the redirect carries `via=google`, which only the Google button sets, and the expired link message otherwise. | CONFIRMED (source read) | apps/web/src/app/auth/callback/route.ts; apps/web/src/lib/signup-callback.ts `VIA_PARAM` | github.com/supabase/auth internal/api/verify.go | Integrator (p18/integration review), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| CAN-SPAM transactional messages: a transactional or relationship message "facilitates, completes, or confirms a commercial transaction that the recipient already has agreed to" or delivers goods or services under it; for a mixed message, "If a recipient reasonably interpreting the subject line would likely conclude that the message contains an advertisement or promotion for a commercial product or service ... the primary purpose of the message is commercial"; opt outs are honored "within 10 business days". lead_results carries no results (they stay in the browser) and links back to the tool, and packs back to a lead links to /signup, so both are marketing: footer, address, unsubscribe headers, a marketing unsubscribe stops them, and lead_results needs the consent box. Unsubscribe requests that arrive by reply (the mailto in List-Unsubscribe and the copy on the unsubscribe page) are added by hand within the 10 business days (docs/PENDING.md, Phase 18 founder steps). | CONFIRMED | packages/email/src/templates/lead.ts; due.ts; copy.ts `LEAD_MARKETING_REASON` | ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/can-spam-act-compliance-guide-business | Integrator (p18/integration review), fetched 2026-10-01 |

| API key prefixes: sandbox (test) keys start `sk_test_`, `rk_test_`, `pk_test_`; live keys `sk_live_`, `rk_live_`, `pk_live_`. Each mode has its own keys and objects. Webhook signing secrets are separate per endpoint secrets, not API keys. | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/billing/readiness.ts `stripeKeyMode`, `stripe_key_mode_mismatch` | docs.stripe.com/keys | Implementer (p20/billing-core), fetched 2026-10-01 |
| List all events: `GET /v1/events` goes back up to 30 days; each event is rendered at the API version it was created with (its `api_version`), not the caller's. Filters: `types` (up to 20 exact names; not together with `type`), `type` (one name or a `*` wildcard), `created` with `gt`, `gte`, `lt`, `lte`, `delivery_success` (false returns events still pending or failed on some endpoint). `limit` 1 to 100, default 10; cursors `starting_after` and `ending_before`; results newest first (the next page is `starting_after` the last id). Installed SDK: `EventListParams { created?: RangeQueryParam \| number; delivery_success?; type?; types? }`. The reconciler passes the 13 handled types in one call (chunked at 20 if more are added), `created.gte` = now minus `billingReconcile.lookbackHours`, `limit` 100, and no `delivery_success` filter, because an event Stripe delivered can still have lost its effect (the P20-02 acceptance case). Whether a `created.gte` older than 30 days errors or returns empty is not stated (UNVERIFIED); the seed keeps the lookback at 72 hours and a seed test keeps it under 30 days. | CONFIRMED (V-bill 1 re-read) | apps/web/src/lib/billing/reconcile.ts `listEvents`, `STRIPE_EVENT_TYPES_PER_LIST`, `STRIPE_LIST_PAGE_SIZE` | docs.stripe.com/api/events/list; installed stripe 18.5.0 types/EventsResource.d.ts | Implementer (p20/billing-core), fetched 2026-10-01 |

## PHASE_20 lane 3: nightly backup and restore drill (checked 2026-10-01, branch p20/data)

Items P20-10 and P20-11 (docs/phases/PHASE_20.md, docs/ops/BACKUP_RESTORE.md). Method: each page below was fetched on 2026-10-01 and read as text, or its source read from the vendor's repository; rows marked "research note" were read the same day for docs/phases/PHASE_20.md (research_notes/Curvi phase 20 readiness/verify_data_and_hosting.md, the founder's local copy) and are cited from there. No live call was made to Supabase, R2, Render or healthchecks.io.

| Fact | Status | Where the code relies on it | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| pg_dump: `--format=custom` writes a compressed archive for pg_restore; `-n`/`--schema` may repeat; `-t`/`--table` may repeat, and "The `-n` and `-N` switches have no effect when `-t` is used", so the auth tables need their own run; `--data-only` dumps "Table data, large objects, and sequence values"; `--no-owner`; `--no-privileges` prevents dumping "access privileges (grant/revoke commands)"; "pg_dump cannot dump from PostgreSQL servers newer than its own major version; it will refuse to even try". | CONFIRMED | ops/cron/backup.sh (two pg_dump runs); ops/cron/Dockerfile `PG_MAJOR`; the schema always comes from migrations (docs/ops/BACKUP_RESTORE.md) | postgresql.org/docs/current/app-pgdump.html | Implementer (p20/data) |
| pg_restore: `-f`/`--file`, "Use - for stdout"; `--data-only` restores "Table data, large objects, and sequence values"; `-n` limits to a schema; `-l`/`--list` prints the table of contents; `--single-transaction` implies `--exit-on-error`; `--disable-triggers` needs a superuser, so the drill uses `session_replication_role` instead (next row). The source, not the page, gives the formats backup.sh parses: `--list` prints `;     Dumped from database version: %s` and `;     Dumped by pg_dump version: %s`, and table data is written as `COPY <schema>.<table> (<columns>) FROM stdin;`, one line per row, ending with `\.`. | CONFIRMED | ops/cron/backup.sh `toc_value` and the manifest awk (fixture in apps/web/scripts/backup-script.test.ts); the drill (P20-11) | postgresql.org/docs/current/app-pgrestore.html; github.com/postgres/postgres REL_17_STABLE src/bin/pg_dump/pg_backup_archiver.c (PrintTOCSummary) and pg_dump.c (dumpTableData) | Implementer (p20/data) |
| Supabase's own restore runs `psql --single-transaction --variable ON_ERROR_STOP=1 ... --command 'SET session_replication_role = replica' --file data.sql`; its data dump uses COPY. | CONFIRMED | the restore drill's load step (P20-11) | supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/migrating-within-supabase/backup-restore | Implementer (p20/data) |
| Connecting pg_dump: the direct host is IPv6 only on Free; the session pooler (`postgres.<ref>` user, port 5432) is IPv4 on every plan and is the backup guide's default. Curvi's server major (`SHOW server_version;`) is not known to the agents; the image defaults to 17 because new projects run 17. | CONFIRMED (research note V-data 2); UNVERIFIED (server major, founder step 1 in BACKUP_RESTORE.md) | `BACKUP_DATABASE_URL`; ops/cron/Dockerfile | research note V-data 2 (supabase.com/docs/guides/database/connecting-to-postgres; supabase/cli config.toml template) | Implementer (p20/data) |
| Supabase Auth tables: the auth server's migrations create `users`, `identities`, `sessions`, `refresh_tokens`, `mfa_factors`, `mfa_challenges`, `mfa_amr_claims`, `flow_state` and `one_time_tokens` (plus SSO and SAML tables). A restore needs `auth.users`, `auth.identities` and `auth.mfa_factors` (logins, linked providers, TOTP factors and, since 2026-08-24, recovery code factors); the rest are sessions and short lived tokens and are never dumped. Whether the pooler's `postgres.<ref>` user may read `auth.mfa_factors` is settled by the first live run. | CONFIRMED (table list); UNVERIFIED (read permission) | ops/cron/backup.sh auth dump | github.com/supabase/auth/tree/master/migrations (listing via api.github.com, 2026-10-01) | Implementer (p20/data) |
| age: `age [--encrypt] (-r RECIPIENT \| -R PATH)... [--armor] [-o OUTPUT] [INPUT]` and `age --decrypt [-i PATH]... [-o OUTPUT] [INPUT]`; input defaults to standard input; public keys `age1...`, secret keys `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1...`; install with `apt install age` on Debian 12 and `brew install age` on macOS. | CONFIRMED | ops/cron/backup.sh (`age --encrypt --recipient ... --output`); the drill (`age --decrypt --identity`) | github.com/FiloSottile/age (README) | Implementer (p20/data) |
| Debian bookworm packages: age 1.1.1, rclone 1.60.1, curl 7.88.1. The official image tag `postgres:17-bookworm` exists. | CONFIRMED | ops/cron/Dockerfile | packages.debian.org/bookworm/{age,rclone,curl}; hub.docker.com/v2/repositories/library/postgres/tags | Implementer (p20/data) |
| rclone with R2: `type = s3`, `provider = Cloudflare`, `endpoint = https://<accountid>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com`, `acl = private`; "Ensure you are running rclone v1.59 or greater"; with an object level token "add `no_check_bucket = true`". A remote can be defined only by environment variables: `RCLONE_CONFIG_` + remote name + `_` + option, all upper case. | CONFIRMED | ops/cron/backup.sh (`RCLONE_CONFIG_CURVIBACKUP_*`, `rclone copyto`) | developers.cloudflare.com/r2/examples/rclone/; rclone docs.md (github.com/rclone/rclone, "Config file") | Implementer (p20/data) |

| R2 lifecycle: rules filter by prefix; S3 shape `{ ID, Status: "Enabled", Filter: { Prefix }, Expiration: { Days } }` and `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload: { DaysAfterInitiation }`; buckets already abort multipart uploads after 7 days; objects are "typically removed ... within 24 hours" of expiry. Wrangler's `lifecycle add` takes `--expire-days` and `--abort-multipart-days`; the file format of `lifecycle set --file` is not documented, so ops/r2/backups-lifecycle.json uses the documented S3 shape and BACKUP_RESTORE.md gives the `lifecycle add` commands. | CONFIRMED; NUANCE (wrangler file format) | ops/r2/backups-lifecycle.json; apps/web/scripts/backup-bucket.test.ts | developers.cloudflare.com/r2/buckets/object-lifecycles/; developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/commands/r2/ | Implementer (p20/data) |

## PHASE_20 (checked 2026-10-01)

### Lane 4 Observe, p20/observe: Sentry (P20-13)

Method: Sentry's docs pages were fetched on 2026-10-01 (the research note `research_notes/Curvi phase 20 readiness/verify_ops_vendors.md` section 1 holds the quotes), the npm registry was read with `npm view`, and the installed `@sentry/nextjs` 11.2.0 and `@sentry/core` 11.2.0 type definitions and sources were read in node_modules. Facts marked "test" are pinned by a test that runs the real SDK (apps/web/src/lib/sentry/runtime.test.ts: a fake DSN, every event read in `beforeSend` and never sent).

| Fact | Value as checked | Status | Used in | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Package | `@sentry/nextjs` 11.2.0 (registry modified 2026-10-01), license MIT, peer `next` `^14.0 \|\| ^15.0.0-rc.0 \|\| ^16.0.0-0`, engines `node` `>=20.19.0 <22.0.0 \|\| >=22.12.0 <23.0.0 \|\| >=23.2.0`. Installed Next is 15.5.26; Render and CI run Node 22 (`NODE_VERSION` 22 in render.yaml, `node-version: 22` in CI), so the Node 22 line must be 22.12 or later. | CONFIRMED | apps/web/package.json `^11.2.0` | `npm view @sentry/nextjs version license peerDependencies engines` | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Instrumentation | `instrumentation.ts` (project root or `src`) with `register()` importing `./sentry.server.config` when `NEXT_RUNTIME` is `nodejs` and `./sentry.edge.config` when it is `edge`, and `export const onRequestError = Sentry.captureRequestError` ("requires `@sentry/nextjs` version `8.28.0` or higher and Next.js 15"). | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/instrumentation.ts, sentry.server.config.ts, sentry.edge.config.ts | docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/manual-setup/ | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| `withSentryConfig` import path | SDK 11 exports `withSentryConfig` from `@sentry/nextjs/config`, not from `@sentry/nextjs`: the manual setup page's example reads `import { withSentryConfig } from "@sentry/nextjs/config";`, and the package's main entry (build/types/index.types.d.ts) no longer exports it (build/types/config/index.d.ts does). Importing it from the main entry fails at load ("withSentryConfig is not a function"). | CONFIRMED (new; the research note did not record the path) | apps/web/next.config.ts | docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/manual-setup/; node_modules/@sentry/nextjs/package.json exports `./config` | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Build options | `org`, `project`, `authToken` (env `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`), `silent`, `widenClientFileUpload` (default false), `release.name`, `telemetry` (the build plugin's own usage telemetry, default true; set to false here), `tunnelRoute` (`string \| boolean`; a fixed string here, `/monitoring`). With middleware, the tunnel path must be excluded from the matcher. Source maps upload only on `next build`; `sourcemaps.deleteSourcemapsAfterUpload` defaults to true for client maps. | CONFIRMED | apps/web/next.config.ts; apps/web/src/middleware-matcher.test.ts | docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/configuration/build/; @sentry/core 11.2.0 build/types/build-time-plugins/buildTimeOptionsBase.d.ts | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| `sendDefaultPii` is gone | The plan said to set `sendDefaultPii: false` because its default was unverified. In SDK 11 the option no longer exists. Sentry's options page: "In version 10 of the SDK, the defaults below apply only when you set the `dataCollection` option. Without it, the deprecated `sendDefaultPii` option controls data collection. Starting with version 11, the defaults always apply." The defaults collect everything: `userInfo` true, `cookies` true, `httpHeaders` request and response true, `httpBodies` all four directions, `urlQueryParams` true, `stackFrameVariables` true, `databaseQueryData` true, `genAI` inputs and outputs true, `queues` true, `frameContextLines` 5. So the init sets `dataCollection` explicitly: every one of those off, request headers limited to the scrubber's allowlist (`{ allow: [...] }`), response headers off. | CONFIRMED; NUANCE (deviation from the plan's setting, same intent) | apps/web/src/lib/sentry/options.ts `DATA_COLLECTION`; options.test.ts; runtime.test.ts (no frame carries `vars`, no `user`) | docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/configuration/options/; @sentry/core 11.2.0 build/types/types/datacollection.d.ts | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Console capture | `captureConsoleIntegration({ levels, handled })` is exported for the Node and edge runtimes; `handled` defaults to true. In @sentry/core 11.2.0 a console call whose arguments hold an `Error` is captured with `captureException(error)`; any other call with `captureMessage(args joined)`; the raw arguments go into `extra.arguments` (so the scrubber walks `extra`). Only `levels: ["error"]` is used, so warnings stay in the log. | CONFIRMED (source and test) | apps/web/src/lib/sentry/options.ts; scrub.ts; runtime.test.ts | @sentry/core 11.2.0 build/esm/integrations/captureconsole.js; research note section 1 | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| One exception, one event | `Client.captureException` returns early for an exception object it already captured (`checkOrSetAlreadyCaught`). A settle failure reported with its job tags is therefore not sent a second time when the runner then logs the same error object with `console.error`. | CONFIRMED (source and test) | apps/web/src/lib/sentry/jobs.ts `reportSettleFailure`; runtime.test.ts "reports a failed settle once" | @sentry/core 11.2.0 build/esm/client.js | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Scope across awaits | `withScope` forks the current scope through Node's async context: tags set on it reach a `console.error` captured after an `await` or inside a timer started within it, so a pack's crash, its time cap line and any `console.error` in the pipeline carry `job_id`, `workspace_id`, `run_key` and `run_kind`. | CONFIRMED (test) | apps/web/src/lib/jobs/inline-runner.ts `inJobContext`; lib/sentry/jobs.ts; runtime.test.ts | runtime.test.ts against the real SDK | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Repeats | The default `dedupeIntegration` drops an event identical to the one just before it, so a hot loop of the very same message sends one event; repeats that differ (an attempt number, an id) pass it and are held by the seeded per error limit (`errorReporting.maxSameErrorPerHour`, ids and numbers normalized out of the fingerprint). | CONFIRMED (test) | apps/web/src/lib/sentry/limits.ts; runtime.test.ts "sends the very same message once in a row" and "sends a repeated error at most maxSameErrorPerHour times" | runtime.test.ts | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Developer plan | Free: "5k errors", "Limited to one user", "Alerts and notifications via email", "30-day lookback", 1 cron monitor, 1 uptime monitor. Team "$26/mo" billed annually, 50k errors. | CONFIRMED | seed `errorReporting` (150 a day keeps 31 days under 5k); founder decision 13 | sentry.io/pricing/ (research note section 1) | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| A build without `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` | Settled by a local `next build` on 2026-10-01 (the Playwright web server build, `CI=1`, no token in the environment): the build succeeds. The plugin prints, for the Node.js, Edge and Client builds, "Warning: No auth token provided. Will not create release." and "Will not upload source maps.", and once "The following packages are marked as external in your bundler configuration but need to be bundled for Sentry instrumentation to work: postgres", which concerns tracing spans for postgres queries, unused here (errors only). CI and the e2e build therefore need no Sentry secret. | CONFIRMED (local build) | apps/web/next.config.ts; CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml unchanged) | local `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/health.spec.ts` build log | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Past the quota | What the Developer plan does past 5k errors (dropped or throttled, spike protection defaults) is not documented for that plan. The seeded caps keep the app under it, so the answer does not change the code. | UNVERIFIED | seed `errorReporting`; lib/sentry/limits.ts | docs.sentry.io/pricing/quotas/ (no Developer specific answer) | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |

### Lane 4 Observe, p20/observe: health status (P20-15)

| Fact | Value as checked | Status | Used in | Source | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Supabase Free database size | "Free Plan projects enter read-only mode when your **database size** exceeds 500 MB"; database size "Displays the actual size of the data within your Postgres database" (data, indexes and materialized views, not the WAL); read only errors carry SQLSTATE 25006. The page's own query is `select pg_size_pretty(sum(pg_database_size(pg_database.datname))) from pg_database;`, so config-health reads `sum(pg_database_size(datname))` over `pg_database` (PGlite answers it too). The seed limit is 500 x 1024 x 1024 bytes, the unit `pg_size_pretty` prints as MB; db_size_high fires at 70 percent of it (`healthLimits`). | CONFIRMED | apps/web/src/lib/config-health.ts `readDatabaseSize`, db_size_high; seed `healthLimits` | supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/database-size (source database-size.mdx, fetched 2026-10-01); research note verify_data_and_hosting.md section 1 | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |
| Who can run that query | Supabase documents the query for the SQL editor, which runs as `postgres`, the same role the app's pooler connection uses. Whether every database in `pg_database` grants it CONNECT (pg_database_size needs CONNECT or pg_read_all_stats) is not documented. A refused read is logged and leaves `databaseSize` null without a warning, so the check fails open. Check once after the deploy: the detailed `/api/health` report (bearer `CRON_SECRET`) shows `databaseSize` with a number. | UNVERIFIED (one production read settles it) | apps/web/src/lib/config-health.ts | supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/database-size | Lane 4 (p20/observe) |

| PostHog `capture_pageview`: `true` captures only the initial pageview of a page load; `"history_change"` captures the initial pageview and a pageview whenever the pathname changes, through the browser's history API, which is what an App Router app needs. The installed types say `true`: "Capture the initial pageview" and `'history_change'`: "Capture the initial pageview and pageviews when the pathname changes". The installed bundle tracks path changes only for `"history_change"` (its `pv()` returns `{path: true}` for it and `{}` for `true`). The config docs say `history_change` "is useful for single page apps" and becomes the default with `defaults: '2025-05-24'` or later. | apps/web/src/components/analytics.tsx | installed @posthog/types 1.412.4 dist/posthog-config.d.ts lines 1317 to 1326; posthog-js 1.434.16 dist/module.js; posthog.com/docs/libraries/js/config, fetched 2026-10-01 | Audit fixer (audit/web-ui), 2026-10-01 |

### Phase 21 transport, database and analytics contracts, checked 2026-10-02

These checks support the implementation candidate. They do not establish a deployed commit, a live receiver activation or completion of the prerequisite release gates. No provider pricing or new paid service is introduced.

| Contract | Official source and verified behavior | Implementation implication |
|---|---|---|
| HTTPS connection and DNS resolution | [Node HTTPS](https://nodejs.org/api/https.html), [DNS](https://nodejs.org/api/dns.html), and [Node 22 net](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/net.html) document HTTPS request options, `lookup` with all answers, `BlockList`, address families and default automatic family selection. The webhook owner verified these references. An independent fixture on Node 22.23.3 confirmed default HTTPS lookup receives `all:true`; it stopped with a synthetic lookup error before a socket connection. | Check every resolved address, pin one vetted family/address with `autoSelectFamily:false`, retain the original TLS hostname and refuse redirects. DNS, connection, response size and total request duration are bounded. The fixture does not contact a receiver. |
| Signing and encryption primitives | [Node 22 crypto](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/crypto.html) documents HMAC, constant-time equality, HKDF and authenticated AES-GCM operations, verified by the webhook owner. | Sign exact request bytes with timestamp and key ID, compare fixed-length signatures, and seal endpoint secrets with a separate purpose using existing server key configuration. Tests use synthetic keys. No persistent credential was created or read for this verification. |
| Outbox locks and queue claims | [PostgreSQL 17 explicit locking](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/explicit-locking.html) confirms that `FOR KEY SHARE` permits `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` while preventing deletion; [SELECT](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-select.html) documents `SKIP LOCKED`. Schema and webhook owners verified both references. | Lock endpoint keys during terminal fanout, use endpoint revisions to reject stale deliveries, and claim deliveries with row locks. This permits a pack terminal update while a sender holds an endpoint lock. Real PostgreSQL concurrency fixtures remain a separate executed-check requirement. |
| Function snapshots and migration locking | [PostgreSQL 17 function volatility](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/xfunc-volatility.html) describes snapshot visibility for stable and volatile functions; [ALTER TABLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-altertable.html) documents table rewriting for volatile defaults. Verified by the schema owner. | The reservation function takes the workspace lock before its accounting query. Migration 0047 adds a random logical-run identity and can rewrite `generation_jobs`; inspect table size and use bounded migration lock/statement timeouts before live application. Existing media and ledger rows are not deleted. |

The review lane also fetched the official [PostHog JavaScript configuration](https://posthog.com/docs/libraries/js/config), [autocapture guide](https://posthog.com/docs/product-analytics/autocapture), [session replay privacy controls](https://posthog.com/docs/session-replay/privacy), and [JavaScript usage reference](https://posthog.com/docs/libraries/js/usage) using read-only HTTPS requests. The web text renderer rejected the site's Markdown content type; the fallback fetched and inspected the official page HTML. These sources confirm that `autocapture:false` leaves separately configured pageviews/pageleaves available, `disable_session_recording:true` opts users out of recording, and `ph-no-capture` blocks recording and autocapture for the marked subtree. The usage reference documents `ph-mask` as the default text-masking class. Cross-checking installed `posthog-js` 1.434.16 confirmed the same option and class behavior.

The resulting privacy policy is explicit consented pageviews and application events, with automatic DOM capture and session recording disabled locally. Case messages, private operator notes and one-time API/webhook secrets additionally receive blocking/masking boundaries. This corrects a concrete data-exposure risk; it makes no claim that sensitive content had actually been sent. Runtime consent, SDK initialization and rendering regression tests are tracked in the Phase 21 review handoff.

### Render build memory, checked 2026-10-02

The release build at `8b594640` first exhausted its approximately 2 GiB V8 heap during compilation. A build-only 4 GiB heap allowed compilation and page-data collection, but Render then stopped static generation after total build memory exceeded 8 GB. The fix bounds build concurrency within the existing pipeline resources.

| Verified behavior | Implementation and evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Render's default Starter build pipeline has 2 CPUs and 8 GB RAM on compute separate from the running service. | Keep the existing runtime plan. The Blueprint installs devDependencies and applies the 4 GiB heap only to `pnpm run build`, preserving other Node options. [Render build pipeline](https://render.com/docs/build-pipeline) |
| Next 15 supports a separate Webpack build worker and reduced-memory compilation; custom Webpack configuration otherwise disables the worker by default. | Enable `webpackBuildWorker` and `webpackMemoryOptimizations`, retaining Sentry and native-module externals. [Next 15 memory guide](https://nextjs.org/docs/15/app/guides/memory-usage) |
| Installed Next 15.5.26 defaults page-worker count to host CPU count minus one. Page analysis and export use separate pools; analysis workers remain until export finishes. Compiler workers end after each compiler. | Set `experimental.cpus: 1` to bound both page-worker pools. Confirmed in installed `next/dist/server/config-shared.js`, `next/dist/build/index.js`, `next/dist/export/index.js` and `next/dist/build/webpack-build/index.js`. Sentry 11.2.0 preserves these experimental options, and compiler workers reload the full custom configuration. |

This change retains TypeScript checking, source maps, scanner settings and runtime job concurrency. A clean production build and browser suite must verify compatibility and memory behavior; configuration inspection alone does not establish successful deployment.

Focused validation on Node 22.23.3 passed: 18 tests across the existing security-header, Sentry configuration and deployment configuration suites; ESLint for `next.config.ts`; web TypeScript; and `git diff --check`. The clean production build then completed in 50.08 seconds. Sampling the build and descendants every 0.5 seconds measured a peak aggregate RSS of 5,047.86 MiB on macOS. This is local compatibility and memory evidence, not a Linux cgroup measurement or proof of a successful Render deployment. Independent review found no blocking scope or correctness issue; full hosted CI and the actual deployment remain required.
